## Q1. Pradhan Mantri Jan-Dhan Yojana and financial inclusion
**Primary route:** Economy → [Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → Pradhan Mantri Jan-Dhan Yojana and financial inclusion  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation → Pradhan Mantri Jan-Dhan Yojana and financial inclusion  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q1 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-pradhan-mantri-jan-dhan-yojana-and-financial-inclusion`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

'Pradhan Mantri Jan-Dhan Yojana' has been launched for

(a) providing housing loan to poor people at cheaper interest rates  
(b) promoting women's Self-Help Groups in backward areas  
(c) promoting financial inclusion in the country  
(d) providing financial help to the marginalized communities  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — promoting financial inclusion in the country**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Pradhan Mantri Jan-Dhan Yojana' (PMJDY) was launched on August 28, 2014, with the primary objective of ensuring access to financial services, namely, banking/savings & deposit accounts, remittance, credit, insurance, and pension in an affordable manner. Its core aim is to achieve comprehensive financial inclusion for all households across the country, especially those in rural and unbanked areas, by providing them with a basic bank account. Therefore, promoting financial inclusion in the country is the direct and overarching purpose of the scheme.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — promoting financial inclusion in the country. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: providing housing loan to poor people at cheaper interest rates** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: promoting women's Self-Help Groups in backward areas** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: promoting financial inclusion in the country** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: providing financial help to the marginalized communities** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **pradhan mantri jan-dhan yojana and financial inclusion**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q2. The Fourteenth Finance Commission, which of the following statements is/are correct?
**Primary route:** Polity → [Finance Commission](../../topics/polity/finance-commission.md) → The Fourteenth Finance Commission, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Finance Commission → The Fourteenth Finance Commission, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q2 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Finance-Commission.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Finance-Commission.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-the-fourteenth-finance-commission-which-of-the-following-statements-is-are-correct`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Fourteenth Finance Commission, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It has increased the share of States in the central divisible pool from 32 percent to 42 percent.  
2. It has made recommendations concerning sector-specific grants.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Fourteenth Finance Commission (FFC), chaired by Dr. Y. V. Reddy, submitted its report in December 2014, covering the period 2015-2020. Statement 1 is correct: A significant recommendation of the FFC was to increase the share of States in the net proceeds of Union taxes from 32 percent to 42 percent, which was the largest ever increase. This was a major step towards enhancing fiscal federalism. Statement 2 is incorrect: The FFC consciously moved away from providing sector-specific grants. Instead, it recommended larger untied transfers to States, giving them greater flexibility in designing and implementing their own development programs. This approach aimed to enhance the autonomy and accountability of the States.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **the fourteenth finance commission, which of the following statements is/are correct?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q4. A decrease in tax to GDP ratio of a country indicates which of the following?
**Primary route:** Economy → [Taxation, GST, Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism](../../topics/economy/taxation-gst-finance-commission-and-fiscal-federalism.md) → A decrease in tax to GDP ratio of a country indicates which of the following?  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Taxation, GST, Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism → A decrease in tax to GDP ratio of a country indicates which of the following?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q4 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-a-decrease-in-tax-to-gdp-ratio-of-a-country-indicates-which-of-the-following`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

A decrease in tax to GDP ratio of a country indicates which of the following?  
1. Slowing economic growth rate  
2. Less equitable distribution of national income  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

A decrease in the tax-to-GDP ratio of a country indicates that the rate of growth of tax collections is slower than the rate of growth of the Gross Domestic Product (GDP). This can indeed be a sign of a slowing economic growth rate, as a sluggish economy might lead to lower incomes, reduced consumption, and thus, lower tax revenues relative to the overall economic output. It could also point to inefficiencies in the tax collection system or tax avoidance. However, a decrease in the tax-to-GDP ratio does not directly indicate less equitable distribution of national income. Income distribution is typically measured by indicators like the Gini coefficient or income quintiles, which assess how income is spread across different segments of the population, independent of the overall tax collection efficiency relative to GDP.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **a decrease in tax to gdp ratio of a country indicates which of the following?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q005"></a>
## Q5. In the South Atlantic and South-Eastern Pacific regions in tropical latitudes, cyclone...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Weather Elements](../../topics/geography/weather-elements.md) → In the South Atlantic and South-Eastern Pacific regions in tropical latitudes, cyclone...  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Weather Elements → In the South Atlantic and South-Eastern Pacific regions in tropical latitudes, cyclone...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q5 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/13_Weather-Elements.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-in-the-south-atlantic-and-south-eastern-pacific-regions-in-tropical-latitudes-cyclone`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the South Atlantic and South-Eastern Pacific regions in tropical latitudes, cyclone does not originate. What is the reason?

(a) Sea surface temperatures are low  
(b) Inter-Tropical Convergence Zone seldom occurs  
(c) Coriolis force is too weak  
(d) Absence of land in those regions  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Inter-Tropical Convergence Zone seldom occurs**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Tropical cyclones require several specific conditions to form: warm ocean waters (at least 26.5°C down to a depth of 50 meters), high relative humidity in the lower-to-mid troposphere, low vertical wind shear, and, crucially, a pre-existing near-surface disturbance or area of convergence, typically within the Inter-Tropical Convergence Zone (ITCZ). The ITCZ is a belt of low pressure where the trade winds from the Northern and Southern Hemispheres converge, leading to rising air and cloud formation, which provides the necessary instability for cyclone development. In the South Atlantic and South-Eastern Pacific regions in tropical latitudes, the ITCZ seldom occurs with sufficient intensity or persistence to foster tropical cyclogenesis. While sea surface temperatures can sometimes be a limiting factor, the primary reason is the lack of consistent atmospheric convergence provided by a robust ITCZ, often coupled with unfavorable wind shear patterns or other atmospheric conditions.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Inter-Tropical Convergence Zone seldom occurs. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Sea surface temperatures are low** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: Inter-Tropical Convergence Zone seldom occurs** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Coriolis force is too weak** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Absence of land in those regions** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **in the south atlantic and south-eastern pacific regions in tropical latitudes, cyclone...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

**Primary route:** Geography → [Indian Political Geography, Boundaries and Neighbours](../../topics/geography/indian-political-geography-boundaries-and-neighbours.md) → Pairs of States of India indicates the easternmost and westernmost State?

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following pairs of States of India indicates the easternmost and westernmost State?

(a) Assam and Rajasthan  
(b) Arunachal Pradesh and Rajasthan  
(c) Assam and Gujarat  
(d) Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

To identify the easternmost and westernmost states of India, one needs to refer to a political map of India. Arunachal Pradesh is located in the extreme eastern part of India, with its easternmost point being near Kibithu. Gujarat is located in the extreme western part of India, with its westernmost point being near Ghuar Mota in the Kutch district. Therefore, the pair Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat correctly indicates the easternmost and westernmost states, respectively.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **B: Arunachal Pradesh and Rajasthan** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Assam and Gujarat** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **pairs of states of india indicates the easternmost and westernmost state?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q007"></a>
## Q7. The Directive Principles of State Policy
**Primary route:** Polity → [Directive Principles](../../topics/polity/directive-principles.md) → The Directive Principles of State Policy  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Directive Principles → The Directive Principles of State Policy  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q7 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-the-directive-principles-of-state-policy`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements regarding the Directive Principles of State Policy:  
1. The Principles spell out the socio-economic democracy in the country.  
2. The provisions contained in these Principles are not enforceable by any court.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: The Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP) are a unique feature of the Indian Constitution, enshrined in Part IV. They aim to establish a 'welfare state' in India by laying down guidelines for the State to promote the socio-economic welfare of its citizens. They reflect the aspirations of the framers of the Constitution for a just and equitable society, thus spelling out the socio-economic democracy in the country. Statement 2 is correct: Article 37 of the Constitution clearly states that 'the provisions contained in this Part shall not be enforceable by any court, but the principles therein laid down are nevertheless fundamental in the governance of the country and it shall be the duty of the State to apply these principles in making laws.' This means that citizens cannot approach a court to compel the government to implement a DPSP, distinguishing them from Fundamental Rights which are justiciable.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — Both 1 and 2. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **the directive principles of state policy**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the 'Index of Eight Core Industries', which one of the following is given the highest weight?

(a) Coal production  
(b) Electricity generation  
(c) Fertilizer production  
(d) Steel production  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Electricity generation**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Index of Eight Core Industries' is a production volume index that measures the performance of eight key infrastructure industries in India: Coal, Crude Oil, Natural Gas, Refinery Products, Fertilizers, Steel, Cement, and Electricity. These industries together account for a significant portion of the Index of Industrial Production (IIP). Among these eight, electricity generation typically holds the highest weightage in the index, reflecting its critical role as an input for almost all other economic activities and its substantial contribution to industrial output. The weightages are revised periodically, but electricity has consistently remained the highest or among the highest.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Electricity generation. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **A: Coal production** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: Electricity generation** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Fertilizer production** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Steel production** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following National Parks is unique in being a swamp with floating vegetation that supports a rich biodiversity?

(a) Bhitarkanika National Park  
(b) Keibul Lamjao National Park  
(c) Keoladeo Ghana National Park  
(d) Sultanpur National Park  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Keibul Lamjao National Park**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Keibul Lamjao National Park is located in the Bishnupur district of Manipur, India, and is the world's only floating national park. It is unique due to the presence of 'phumdis', which are heterogeneous masses of vegetation, soil, and organic matter in various stages of decomposition, floating on the surface of Loktak Lake. These phumdis support a rich biodiversity, including the endangered Sangai (brow-antlered deer), which is endemic to this floating habitat. The other national parks mentioned do not have this unique characteristic of floating vegetation.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **C: Keoladeo Ghana National Park** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Sultanpur National Park** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

Which of the following statements is/are correct regarding National Innovation Foundation-India (NIF)?  
1. NIF is an autonomous body of the Department of Science and Technology under the Central Government.

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: The National Innovation Foundation-India (NIF) is indeed an autonomous body of the Department of Science and Technology (DST), Government of India. It was set up in 2000 with the mission to help India become a creative and knowledge-based society by fostering grassroots innovations and outstanding traditional knowledge. Statement 2 is incorrect: NIF's primary initiative is to strengthen and scout for technological innovations from grassroots innovators and outstanding traditional knowledge holders across the country, as well as to help in their value addition, commercialization, and intellectual property protection. While it promotes scientific research, its focus is distinctly on grassroots and informal sector innovations, not primarily on highly advanced scientific research in premier scientific institutions in collaboration with highly advanced foreign scientific institutions.

## Q11. What can be the impact of excessive/inappropriate use of nitrogenous fertilizers in...
**Primary route:** Economy → [Irrigation, Inputs, Credit, Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture](../../topics/economy/irrigation-inputs-credit-insurance-and-sustainable-agriculture.md) → What can be the impact of excessive/inappropriate use of nitrogenous fertilizers in...  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Irrigation, Inputs, Credit, Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture → What can be the impact of excessive/inappropriate use of nitrogenous fertilizers in...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q11 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-what-can-be-the-impact-of-excessive-inappropriate-use-of-nitrogenous-fertilizers-in`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What can be the impact of excessive/inappropriate use of nitrogenous fertilizers in agriculture?  
1. Proliferation of nitrogen-fixing microorganisms in soil can occur.  
2. Increase in the acidity of soil can take place.  
3. Leaching of nitrate to the groundwater can occur.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. Proliferation of nitrogen-fixing microorganisms in soil can occur: This is incorrect. Excessive application of nitrogenous fertilizers can actually suppress the activity of natural nitrogen-fixing microorganisms (like rhizobia in legumes) because the plants have an ample supply of readily available nitrogen, reducing their need to form symbiotic relationships for nitrogen fixation. Over-fertilization can reduce microbial diversity and activity. 2. Increase in the acidity of soil can take place: This is correct. Many nitrogenous fertilizers, particularly ammonium-based ones (e.g., urea, ammonium sulfate), undergo nitrification in the soil, a process where ammonium (NH4+) is converted to nitrate (NO3-). This process releases hydrogen ions (H+), which increases soil acidity. Prolonged use can lead to soil acidification, impacting nutrient availability and crop growth. 3. Leaching of nitrate to the groundwater can occur: This is correct. Nitrate (NO3-), a highly mobile form of nitrogen, is not readily bound by soil particles. When applied in excess or during heavy rainfall, nitrate can easily leach through the soil profile and enter groundwater, leading to contamination of drinking water sources and contributing to eutrophication in surface water bodies.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 2 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **what can be the impact of excessive/inappropriate use of nitrogenous fertilizers in...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q12. The International Union for Conservation of Nature and Natural Resources (IUCN) and the...
**Primary route:** Environment → [Multilateral Environmental Conventions](../../topics/environment/multilateral-environmental-conventions-cbd-basel-stockholm-montreal.md) → The International Union for Conservation of Nature and Natural Resources (IUCN) and the...  
**Primary classification:** Environment → Multilateral Environmental Conventions → The International Union for Conservation of Nature and Natural Resources (IUCN) and the...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q12 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/22_Multilateral-Environmental-Conventions-CBD-Basel-Stockholm-Montreal.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/22_Multilateral-Environmental-Conventions-CBD-Basel-Stockholm-Montreal.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-the-international-union-for-conservation-of-nature-and-natural-resources-iucn-and-the`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the International Union for Conservation of Nature and Natural Resources (IUCN) and the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora (CITES), which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. IUCN is an organ of the United Nations and CITES is an international agreement between governments.  
2. IUCN runs thousands of field projects around the world to better manage natural environments.  
3. CITES is legally binding on the States that have joined it, but this Convention does not take the place of national laws.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. IUCN is an organ of the United Nations and CITES is an international agreement between governments: This statement is incorrect. The International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN) is an independent international organization (an NGO with a unique observer status at the UN General Assembly), not an organ of the United Nations. CITES (Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora) is indeed an international agreement between governments. 2. IUCN runs thousands of field projects around the world to better manage natural environments: This statement is correct. IUCN actively engages in and supports a vast number of conservation projects globally, working with governments, NGOs, and local communities to implement conservation strategies on the ground. 3. CITES is legally binding on the States that have joined it, but this Convention does not take the place of national laws: This statement is correct. CITES is a legally binding international treaty, meaning States (Parties) that ratify it are legally obliged to implement its provisions. However, CITES itself does not replace national laws. Instead, each Party must enact its own domestic legislation to enforce CITES regulations and apply penalties for violations.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **the international union for conservation of nature and natural resources (iucn) and the...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

The Fair and Remunerative Price (FRP) of sugarcane is the minimum price that sugar mills are legally obligated to pay to sugarcane farmers. This price is approved by the Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA), which is a key decision-making body of the Government of India. The CCEA's decision is typically based on the recommendations provided by the Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices (CACP), which advises the government on minimum support prices (MSPs) and FRPs for various agricultural commodities. However, the final approval authority rests with the CCEA.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q014"></a>
## Q14. What explains the eastward flow of the equatorial counter-current?
**Primary route:** Geography → [Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity](../../topics/geography/oceans-currents-tides-and-salinity.md) → What explains the eastward flow of the equatorial counter-current?  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity → What explains the eastward flow of the equatorial counter-current?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q14 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/12_The-Oceans-Currents-Tides-Salinity.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/12_The-Oceans-Currents-Tides-Salinity.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-what-explains-the-eastward-flow-of-the-equatorial-counter-current`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What explains the eastward flow of the equatorial counter-current?

(a) The Earth's rotation on its axis  
(b) Convergence of the two equatorial currents  
(c) Difference in salinity of water  
(d) Occurrence of the belt of calm near the equator  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Convergence of the two equatorial currents**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Equatorial Counter-Current, or Equatorial Undercurrent, is an eastward-flowing current found near the equator in the Pacific, Atlantic, and Indian Oceans. This current flows against the prevailing westward flow of the North and South Equatorial Currents. Its eastward flow is primarily explained by the accumulation of water in the western part of the ocean basin due to the continuous push of the westward-flowing equatorial currents by the trade winds. This piling up of water creates a hydrostatic pressure gradient, where the sea level is slightly higher in the west. This pressure gradient then drives the water back eastward as a counter-current in the absence of strong Coriolis deflection at the equator. While other factors might subtly influence ocean currents, the convergence and resulting pressure gradient due to the two equatorial currents is the primary mechanism for the eastward counter-current.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Convergence of the two equatorial currents. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The Earth's rotation on its axis** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: Convergence of the two equatorial currents** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Difference in salinity of water** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Occurrence of the belt of calm near the equator** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **what explains the eastward flow of the equatorial counter-current?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q15. Consider the following pairs
**Primary route:** Geography → [Cultural and Social Geography of India](../../topics/geography/cultural-and-social-geography-of-india.md) → Consider the following pairs  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Cultural and Social Geography of India → Consider the following pairs  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q15 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/37_Cultural-and-Social-Geography-of-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/37_Cultural-and-Social-Geography-of-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-consider-the-following-pairs`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:  
Place of Pilgrimage Location  
1. Srisailam : Nallamala Hills  
2. Omkareshwar : Satmala Hills  
3. Pushkar : Mahadeo Hills  
Which of the above pairs is/are correctly matched?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. Srisailam : Nallamala Hills: This pair is correctly matched. Srisailam, a famous Jyotirlinga temple dedicated to Lord Shiva, is situated on the Nallamala Hills of Andhra Pradesh. 2. Omkareshwar : Satmala Hills: This pair is incorrectly matched. Omkareshwar is a prominent Jyotirlinga shrine located on an island in the Narmada River in Madhya Pradesh. It is associated with the Satpura Range, not the Satmala Hills, which are part of the Sahyadri (Western Ghats) in Maharashtra. 3. Pushkar : Mahadeo Hills: This pair is incorrectly matched. Pushkar is a holy town in Rajasthan, famous for its Pushkar Lake and the Brahma Temple. It is located in the Aravalli Range. The Mahadeo Hills are part of the Satpura Range in Madhya Pradesh.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q016"></a>
## Q16. Rowlatt Satyagraha, which of the following statements is/are correct?
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Gandhian Mass Mobilisation](../../topics/modern-history/gandhian-mass-mobilisation.md) → Rowlatt Satyagraha, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Gandhian Mass Mobilisation → Rowlatt Satyagraha, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q16 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/19_Gandhis-Rise-Rowlatt-and-Jallianwala.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/19_Gandhis-Rise-Rowlatt-and-Jallianwala.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-rowlatt-satyagraha-which-of-the-following-statements-is-are-correct`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Rowlatt Satyagraha, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. The Rowlatt Act was based on the recommendations of the 'Sedition Committee'.  
2. In Rowlatt Satyagraha, Gandhiji tried to utilize the Home Rule League.  
3. Demonstrations against the arrival of Simon Commission coincided with Rowlatt Satyagraha.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. The Rowlatt Act was based on the recommendations of the 'Sedition Committee': This statement is correct. The Anarchical and Revolutionary Crimes Act of 1919, popularly known as the Rowlatt Act, was indeed passed based on the recommendations of the Sedition Committee, headed by Sir Sidney Rowlatt. The committee was set up to evaluate the extent of revolutionary conspiracies in India. 2. In Rowlatt Satyagraha, Gandhiji tried to utilize the Home Rule League: This statement is correct. Mahatma Gandhi decided to launch a nationwide Satyagraha against the Rowlatt Act. To organize this, he largely utilized the network of the Home Rule League, whose members were already active and familiar with the idea of mass mobilization and direct action. 3. Demonstrations against the arrival of Simon Commission coincided with Rowlatt Satyagraha: This statement is incorrect. The Rowlatt Satyagraha took place in 1919. The Simon Commission, on the other hand, arrived in India in 1928, nearly a decade later. Therefore, there was no temporal coincidence between the two events.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 1 and 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **rowlatt satyagraha, which of the following statements is/are correct?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q18. Fly ash' produced by the power plants using coal as fuel, which of the following...
**Primary route:** Environment → [Air Pollution and CPCB Standards](../../topics/environment/air-pollution-and-cpcb-standards.md) → Fly ash' produced by the power plants using coal as fuel, which of the following...  
**Primary classification:** Environment → Air Pollution and CPCB Standards → Fly ash' produced by the power plants using coal as fuel, which of the following...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q18 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/13_Air-Pollution-and-CPCB-Standards.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/13_Air-Pollution-and-CPCB-Standards.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-fly-ash-produced-by-the-power-plants-using-coal-as-fuel-which-of-the-following`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'fly ash' produced by the power plants using coal as fuel, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. Fly ash can be used in the production of bricks for building construction.  
2. Fly ash can be used as a replacement for some of the Portland cement contents of concrete.  
3. Fly ash is made up of silicon dioxide and calcium oxide only, and does not contain any toxic elements.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. Fly ash can be used in the production of bricks for building construction: This statement is correct. Fly ash is a common pozzolanic material used to produce fly ash bricks, which are an eco-friendly and often more durable alternative to traditional clay bricks. It helps conserve topsoil and reduces the amount of waste sent to landfills. 2. Fly ash can be used as a replacement for some of the Portland cement contents of concrete: This statement is correct. Fly ash, due to its pozzolanic properties, reacts with the calcium hydroxide in cement hydration to form cementitious compounds. It is widely used as a supplementary cementitious material (SCM) in concrete, improving its strength, durability, and workability, while reducing cement consumption and carbon emissions. 3. Fly ash is made up of silicon dioxide and calcium oxide only, and does not contain any toxic elements: This statement is incorrect. While fly ash is primarily composed of silicon dioxide (SiO2), aluminium oxide (Al2O3), iron oxide (Fe2O3), and calcium oxide (CaO), its composition varies depending on the type of coal burned. Crucially, it often contains various trace elements, some of which can be toxic, such as arsenic, lead, mercury, chromium, and cadmium. Therefore, it is not composed 'only' of silicon dioxide and calcium oxide, and it 'does' contain toxic elements, necessitating careful management and disposal.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 and 2. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **fly ash' produced by the power plants using coal as fuel, which of the following...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q19. Dugong', a mammal found in India, which of the following statements is/are correct?
**Primary route:** Environment → [Coastal and Marine Ecology](../../topics/environment/coastal-and-marine-ecology-crz-blue-economy.md) → Dugong', a mammal found in India, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Primary classification:** Environment → Coastal and Marine Ecology → Dugong', a mammal found in India, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q19 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology-CRZ-Blue-Economy.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/24_Coastal-and-Marine-Ecology-CRZ-Blue-Economy.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-dugong-a-mammal-found-in-india-which-of-the-following-statements-is-are-correct`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'dugong', a mammal found in India, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It is a herbivorous marine animal.  
2. It is found along the entire coast of India.  
3. It is given legal protection under Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

1. It is a herbivorous marine animal: This statement is correct. Dugongs (also known as sea cows) are the only strictly herbivorous marine mammals. They primarily feed on seagrasses found in shallow coastal waters. 2. It is found along the entire coast of India: This statement is incorrect. Dugongs have a fragmented distribution along the Indian coast. Significant populations and habitats in India are found in the Gulf of Mannar, Palk Bay, Gulf of Kutch, and Andaman & Nicobar Islands. They are not found uniformly along the entire coastline, being highly dependent on the presence of seagrass beds. 3. It is given legal protection under Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972: This statement is correct. Dugongs are highly vulnerable to extinction due to habitat loss, hunting, and incidental capture in fishing gear. Recognizing their endangered status, they are accorded the highest level of legal protection under Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972, which offers absolute protection to listed species.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

Who of the following was/were economic critic/critics of colonialism in India?  
1. Dadabhai Naoroji  
2. G. Subramania Iyer  
3. R. C. Dutt

(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All three individuals listed – Dadabhai Naoroji, G. Subramania Iyer, and R. C. Dutt – were prominent economic critics of colonialism in India during the late 19th and early 20th centuries. They meticulously analyzed and exposed the exploitative nature of British economic policies, which they argued were responsible for the impoverishment of India. 1. Dadabhai Naoroji: He is famously known for propounding the 'Drain of Wealth' theory through his book 'Poverty and Un-British Rule in India', arguing how British rule systematically drained India's wealth. 2. G. Subramania Iyer: A prominent journalist and nationalist, he was a vocal critic of the British economic exploitation and advocated for economic self-reliance. He co-founded 'The Hindu' newspaper. 3. R. C. Dutt (Romesh Chunder Dutt): A retired Indian Civil Service (ICS) officer, he authored 'The Economic History of India' (in two volumes), which provided a comprehensive and critical account of the British economic policies and their devastating impact on India's economy.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q022"></a>
## Q22. When the Reserve Bank of India reduces the Statutory Liquidity Ratio by 50 basis points,...
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → When the Reserve Bank of India reduces the Statutory Liquidity Ratio by 50 basis points,...  
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → When the Reserve Bank of India reduces the Statutory Liquidity Ratio by 50 basis points,...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q22 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-when-the-reserve-bank-of-india-reduces-the-statutory-liquidity-ratio-by-50-basis-points`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

When the Reserve Bank of India reduces the Statutory Liquidity Ratio by 50 basis points, which of the following is likely to happen?

(a) India's GDP growth rate increases drastically  
(b) Foreign Institutional Investors may bring more capital into our country  
(c) Scheduled Commercial Banks may cut their lending rates  
(d) It may drastically reduce the liquidity to the banking system  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Scheduled Commercial Banks may cut their lending rates**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR) is a monetary policy tool used by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI). It mandates commercial banks to maintain a certain percentage of their Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) in the form of liquid assets like cash, gold, or approved securities. When the RBI reduces the SLR by 50 basis points (0.50%), it means banks are now required to set aside a smaller proportion of their deposits as reserves. This reduction frees up more funds for the scheduled commercial banks to lend to the public. With increased liquidity and more funds available for credit, banks typically face less pressure on their funds, making them more likely to cut their lending rates to attract borrowers and increase their loan book. This move aims to stimulate credit growth in the economy.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — Scheduled Commercial Banks may cut their lending rates. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: Foreign Institutional Investors may bring more capital into our country** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: Scheduled Commercial Banks may cut their lending rates** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: It may drastically reduce the liquidity to the banking system** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

2. Nanotechnology can largely contribute to gene therapy.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Nanotechnology, through the use of nanoparticles and nanocarriers, enables the precise delivery of drugs to specific cells, tissues, or organs in the body. This targeted approach minimizes the drug's impact on healthy cells, reduces side effects, and often increases the efficacy of the treatment, particularly in areas like cancer therapy. 2. Nanotechnology can largely contribute to gene therapy: This statement is correct. Nanotechnology plays a crucial role in overcoming challenges in gene therapy, primarily in delivering genetic material (like DNA or RNA) into target cells. Nanocarriers can protect the genetic material from degradation, facilitate its entry into cells, and guide it to the correct location within the cell, making gene therapy more efficient and safer.

In India, markets in agricultural products are regulated under the

(b) Agricultural Produce Market Committee Act enacted by States  
(c) Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking) Act, 1937

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Agricultural Produce Market Committee Act enacted by States**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In India, the marketing of agricultural produce is primarily regulated by the Agricultural Produce Market Committee (APMC) Act. These Acts are enacted by individual State Governments. Under these Acts, various agricultural commodities are notified, and specific market areas are designated where trading of these commodities can take place through regulated market yards (mandis). The APMCs are responsible for regulating the sale and purchase of agricultural produce, ensuring fair trade practices, and collecting market fees. Other acts mentioned have different purposes: the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 deals with control over production, supply, and distribution; the Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking) Act, 1937 deals with quality standards; and the Food Products Order, 1956 and Meat and Food Products Order, 1973 relate to food processing standards.

**Statement/item-level result:**

## Q26. Congress Socialist Party, consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements](../../topics/modern-history/left-peasant-workers-and-states-peoples-movements.md) → Congress Socialist Party, consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements → Congress Socialist Party, consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q26 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-congress-socialist-party-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Congress Socialist Party, consider the following statements:  
1. It advocated the boycott of British goods and evasion of taxes.  
2. It wanted to establish the dictatorship of proletariat.  
3. It advocated separate electorate for minorities and oppressed classes.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — None**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Congress Socialist Party (CSP) was formed in 1934 by a group of younger leaders within the Indian National Congress who believed in socialist ideology. Let's analyze each statement: 1. It advocated the boycott of British goods and evasion of taxes: While the CSP participated in the nationalist movement and supported various forms of protest, its primary focus was on establishing a socialist society and transforming the socio-economic structure of India, rather than solely advocating for basic boycott and tax evasion. Such actions were part of broader civil disobedience, but not the defining unique advocacy of the CSP. 2. It wanted to establish the dictatorship of proletariat: The CSP, while socialist in its outlook, did not explicitly advocate for a 'dictatorship of the proletariat' in the strict Marxist-Leninist sense, which implies a violent revolution and a one-party state. They aimed for democratic socialism and worked within the framework of the Indian National Congress, seeking to achieve their goals through constitutional and democratic means (after independence) and mass movements. 3. It advocated separate electorate for minorities and oppressed classes: The CSP was strongly opposed to separate electorates, viewing them as divisive and detrimental to national unity. They believed that such divisions would hinder the struggle for independence and the establishment of a unified socialist society. They championed the cause of the oppressed but within the framework of a united national movement. Based on the above analysis, none of the statements accurately represent the core ideologies and positions of the Congress Socialist Party.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — None. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: None** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **congress socialist party, consider the following statements**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q027"></a>
## Q27. Rajya Sabha powers over Money Bills, Demands for Grants and the Annual Financial Statement
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Rajya Sabha powers over Money Bills, Demands for Grants and the Annual Financial Statement  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Rajya Sabha powers over Money Bills, Demands for Grants and the Annual Financial Statement  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q27 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-rajya-sabha-powers-over-money-bills-demands-for-grants-and-the-annual-financial-statement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:  
1. The Rajya Sabha has no power either to reject or to amend a Money Bill.  
2. The Rajya Sabha cannot vote on the Demands for Grants.  
3. The Rajya Sabha cannot discuss the Annual Financial Statement.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding the powers of the Rajya Sabha: 1. The Rajya Sabha has no power either to reject or to amend a Money Bill: This statement is correct. According to Article 109 of the Constitution, a Money Bill can only be introduced in the Lok Sabha. Once passed by the Lok Sabha, it is sent to the Rajya Sabha for its recommendations. The Rajya Sabha must return the Bill within 14 days, with or without recommendations. The Lok Sabha is not bound to accept these recommendations. If the Rajya Sabha does not return the Bill within 14 days, it is deemed to have been passed by both Houses. Thus, the Rajya Sabha cannot reject or amend a Money Bill. 2. The Rajya Sabha cannot vote on the Demands for Grants: This statement is correct. Demands for Grants are part of the Annual Financial Statement (Budget) and are presented to the Lok Sabha for voting. The Rajya Sabha discusses the Budget, including the Demands for Grants, but it does not have the power to vote on them. Voting on Demands for Grants is the exclusive prerogative of the Lok Sabha. 3. The Rajya Sabha cannot discuss the Annual Financial Statement: This statement is incorrect. The Rajya Sabha has the power to discuss the Annual Financial Statement (Budget) in its entirety. While it cannot vote on the demands for grants, a general discussion on the Budget takes place in both Houses of Parliament. Therefore, statements 1 and 2 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 1 and 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **rajya sabha powers over money bills, demands for grants and the annual financial statement**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q28. Government of India Act of 1919 clearly defined
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development](../../topics/modern-history/colonial-government-structure-and-constitutional-development.md) → Government of India Act of 1919 clearly defined  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development → Government of India Act of 1919 clearly defined  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q28 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/18_WWI-Home-Rule-and-Lucknow-Pact.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/18_WWI-Home-Rule-and-Lucknow-Pact.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-government-of-india-act-of-1919-clearly-defined`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Government of India Act of 1919 clearly defined

(a) the separation of power between the judiciary and the legislature  
(b) the jurisdiction of the central and provincial governments  
(c) the powers of the Secretary of State for India and the Viceroy  
(d) None of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — the jurisdiction of the central and provincial governments**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Government of India Act of 1919, also known as the Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms, was a significant constitutional reform during British rule in India. One of its key features was the introduction of 'dyarchy' in the provinces. To implement this system, the Act clearly demarcated and separated the subjects of administration into two lists: Central and Provincial. The Central subjects remained under the direct control of the Governor-General-in-Council, while the Provincial subjects were further divided into 'reserved' and 'transferred' subjects. This explicit division for the first time clearly defined the jurisdiction of the central and provincial governments, laying the groundwork for federal principles in Indian administration.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — the jurisdiction of the central and provincial governments. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the separation of power between the judiciary and the legislature** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: the jurisdiction of the central and provincial governments** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: the powers of the Secretary of State for India and the Viceroy** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: None of the above** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **government of india act of 1919 clearly defined**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

In India, the Consumer Price Index (CPI) numbers for different categories of workers are compiled by different agencies. ## Q30. In the context of modern scientific research, consider the following statements about...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Physics Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-physics-fundamentals.md) → In the context of modern scientific research, consider the following statements about...  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Physics Fundamentals → In the context of modern scientific research, consider the following statements about...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q30 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-in-the-context-of-modern-scientific-research-consider-the-following-statements-about`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of modern scientific research, consider the following statements about 'IceCube', a particle detector located at South Pole, which was recently in the news:  
1. It is the world's largest neutrino detector, encompassing a cubic kilometre of ice.  
2. It is a powerful telescope to search for dark matter.  
3. It is buried deep in the ice.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

IceCube is a state-of-the-art particle detector located deep within the ice at the South Pole. Let's analyze the given statements: 1. It is the world's largest neutrino detector, encompassing a cubic kilometre of ice: This statement is correct. IceCube consists of thousands of optical sensors embedded in a cubic kilometer of clear Antarctic ice, making it the world's largest neutrino observatory. 2. It is a powerful telescope to search for dark matter: This statement is correct. While its primary mission is to detect high-energy neutrinos from cosmic sources, IceCube's capabilities also allow it to be used in the search for dark matter, specifically by looking for neutrinos produced from dark matter annihilation or decay in the galactic halo or other astronomical objects. 3. It is buried deep in the ice: This statement is correct. The sensors of the IceCube detector are deployed at depths between 1,450 and 2,450 meters (nearly a mile and a half) in the glacial ice at the geographic South Pole. The deep ice provides a stable and ultra-transparent medium necessary for detecting the faint light signals produced by neutrino interactions. Therefore, all three statements are correct regarding the IceCube particle detector.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **in the context of modern scientific research, consider the following statements about...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The terms 'Agreement on Agriculture', 'Agreement on the Application of Sanitary and Phytosanitary Measures' and 'Peace Clause' appear in the news frequently in the context of the affairs of the

(a) Food and Agriculture Organization  
(b) United Nations Framework Conference on Climate Change  
(c) World Trade Organization  
(d) United Nations Environment Programme  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — World Trade Organization**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The terms 'Agreement on Agriculture', 'Agreement on the Application of Sanitary and Phytosanitary Measures', and 'Peace Clause' are all significant concepts and agreements within the framework of international trade, specifically associated with the World Trade Organization (WTO). The Agreement on Agriculture (AoA) is a WTO agreement aimed at reforming trade in the agricultural sector and removing barriers to trade. The Agreement on the Application of Sanitary and Phytosanitary Measures (SPS Agreement) sets out the basic rules for food safety and animal and plant health standards in international trade. The Peace Clause is a provision under the WTO's Agreement on Agriculture that protects developing countries from legal challenges at the WTO if they breach certain subsidy limits, provided they fulfill specific conditions, typically related to food security programs. All these terms are integral to the rules-based multilateral trading system overseen by the WTO.
