## Q32. Near Field Communication (NFC) Technology', which of the following statements is/are...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Computing Fundamentals, Hardware, Software, Networks and Cloud](../../topics/science-and-technology/computing-fundamentals-hardware-software-networks-and-cloud.md) → Near Field Communication (NFC) Technology', which of the following statements is/are...  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Computing Fundamentals, Hardware, Software, Networks and Cloud → Near Field Communication (NFC) Technology', which of the following statements is/are...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q32 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-near-field-communication-nfc-technology-which-of-the-following-statements-is-are`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Near Field Communication (NFC) Technology', which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It is a contactless communication technology that uses electromagnetic radio fields.  
2. NFC is designed for use by devices which can be at a distance of even a metre from each other.  
3. NFC can use encryption when sending sensitive information.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding 'Near Field Communication (NFC) Technology': 1. It is a contactless communication technology that uses electromagnetic radio fields: This statement is correct. NFC operates on the principles of radio-frequency identification (RFID) and uses electromagnetic induction to enable communication between devices that are in close proximity. 2. NFC is designed for use by devices which can be at a distance of even a metre from each other: This statement is incorrect. NFC is specifically designed for very short-range communication, typically limited to a few centimeters (usually 4 cm or less). This close proximity requirement enhances security and prevents accidental communication. 3. NFC can use encryption when sending sensitive information: This statement is correct. While NFC itself does not inherently provide strong encryption, it can be used in conjunction with security protocols and encryption methods (e.g., within payment systems or secure access applications) to protect sensitive data during transmission. The security of NFC transactions often relies on higher-layer protocols and software implementations. Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **near field communication (nfc) technology', which of the following statements is/are...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. ### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Convertibility of rupee implies

(a) being able to convert rupee notes into gold  
(b) allowing the value of rupee to be fixed by market forces  
(c) freely permitting the conversion of rupee to other currencies and vice versa  
(d) developing an international market for currencies in India  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — freely permitting the conversion of rupee to other currencies and vice versa**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Convertibility of a currency, such as the Indian Rupee, refers to the ease with which it can be exchanged for other foreign currencies, and vice versa, without significant governmental restrictions or controls. When a currency is convertible, individuals and businesses can exchange it for foreign currencies for various purposes, including trade, investment, and travel. This implies that the value of the currency is largely determined by market forces (demand and supply) rather than administrative controls. India's Rupee is currently fully convertible on the current account and partially convertible on the capital account.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:  
Medieval Indian State Present Region  
1. Champaka : Central India  
2. Durgara : Jammu  
3. Kuluta : Malabar  
Which of the above pairs is/are correctly matched?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's examine each pair: 1. Champaka : Central India: This pair is incorrectly matched. Champaka, or Chamba, was a medieval Indian state located in the Western Himalayas, primarily corresponding to parts of present-day Himachal Pradesh, not Central India. 2. Durgara : Jammu: This pair is correctly matched. Durgara was an ancient and medieval kingdom that corresponds to the region of Jammu, in what is now Jammu and Kashmir. The Dogras, an ethnic group, trace their origins to this region. 3. Kuluta : Malabar: This pair is incorrectly matched. Kuluta was an ancient kingdom located in the Kullu Valley of Himachal Pradesh, in the Western Himalayas. Malabar, on the other hand, refers to the southwestern coast of India, primarily in present-day Kerala. Therefore, only pair 2 is correctly matched.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following rivers:  
1. Vamsadhara  
2. Indravati  
3. Pranahita  
4. Pennar  
Which of the above are tributaries of Godavari?

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 2, 3 and 4  
(c) 1, 2 and 4  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the given rivers to determine which are tributaries of the Godavari: 1. Vamsadhara: This is an important east-flowing river in Odisha and Andhra Pradesh, flowing directly into the Bay of Bengal. It is an independent river system and not a tributary of the Godavari. 2. Indravati: This is a major tributary of the Godavari River. It originates in the Kalahandi district of Odisha and flows through Chhattisgarh before joining the Godavari. 3. Pranahita: This is the largest tributary of the Godavari River. It is formed by the confluence of the Wardha, Wainganga, and Penganga rivers and joins the Godavari in Telangana. 4. Pennar: Also known as Penneru, this is an independent river system that flows through the states of Karnataka and Andhra Pradesh, ultimately draining into the Bay of Bengal. It is not a tributary of the Godavari. Therefore, only Indravati and Pranahita are tributaries of the Godavari River.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 2 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Canonical slug:** `polity-when-a-bill-is-referred-to-a-joint-sitting-of-both-the-houses-of-the-parliament-it-has`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

When a bill is referred to a joint sitting of both the Houses of the Parliament, it has to be passed by

(a) a simple majority of members present and voting  
(b) three-fourths majority of members present and voting  
(c) two-thirds majority of the Houses  
(d) absolute majority of the Houses  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — a simple majority of members present and voting**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Article 108 of the Indian Constitution provides for a joint sitting of both Houses of Parliament in specific situations of disagreement over a Bill (excluding Money Bills and Constitutional Amendment Bills). If a joint sitting is convened, the Bill is passed by a simple majority of the total number of members of both Houses present and voting. This means that more than half of the members who are present and cast their vote must vote in favor of the Bill for it to be passed. This simple majority rule makes it easier to resolve deadlocks, as the Lok Sabha (which has a larger membership) usually prevails in a joint sitting.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — a simple majority of members present and voting. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: a simple majority of members present and voting** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: three-fourths majority of members present and voting** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: two-thirds majority of the Houses** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: absolute majority of the Houses** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **when a bill is referred to a joint sitting of both the houses of the parliament, it has...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q38. Regions of India has a combination of mangrove forest, evergreen forest and deciduous...
**Primary route:** Environment → [Forest Types and Forest Rights Act](../../topics/environment/forest-types-and-forest-rights-act.md) → Regions of India has a combination of mangrove forest, evergreen forest and deciduous...

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following regions of India has a combination of mangrove forest, evergreen forest and deciduous forest?

(a) North Coastal Andhra Pradesh  
(b) South-West Bengal  
(c) Southern Saurashtra  
(d) Andaman and Nicobar Islands  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Andaman and Nicobar Islands**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Andaman and Nicobar Islands are unique in India due to their geographical location (tropical), island ecosystem, and diverse topography. This allows them to host a combination of different forest types: Mangrove forests: Found extensively along the coasts, tidal creeks, and estuaries, adapting to saline and tidal conditions. Evergreen forests: Dense tropical evergreen forests dominate the islands, characterized by a multi-layered canopy and rich biodiversity, due to high rainfall and humidity. Deciduous forests: Moist deciduous forests, also known as semi-evergreen forests, are present in some areas, particularly in the northern parts of the islands, where there might be a short dry season leading to some leaf shedding. This distinct combination of these three major forest types makes the Andaman and Nicobar Islands stand out as a biodiversity hotspot compared to the other options which might have one or two, but not all three in such prominent and diverse forms.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Andaman and Nicobar Islands. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: South-West Bengal** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Southern Saurashtra** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Andaman and Nicobar Islands** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **regions of india has a combination of mangrove forest, evergreen forest and deciduous...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q039"></a>
## Q39. Kingdoms were associated with the life of the Buddha?
**Primary route:** Ancient History → [Jainism and Buddhism](../../topics/ancient-history/jainism-and-buddhism.md) → Kingdoms were associated with the life of the Buddha?  
**Primary classification:** Ancient History → Jainism and Buddhism → Kingdoms were associated with the life of the Buddha?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q39 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Ancient-Indian-History/basic/10_Jainism-and-Buddhism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Ancient-Indian-History/basic/10_Jainism-and-Buddhism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `ancient-history-kingdoms-were-associated-with-the-life-of-the-buddha`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following kingdoms were associated with the life of the Buddha?  
1. Avanti  
2. Gandhara  
3. Kosala  
4. Magadha  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1, 3 and 4  
(d) 3 and 4 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 3 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the association of each kingdom with the life of Gautama Buddha: 1. Avanti: While Avanti was one of the sixteen Mahajanapadas and a significant kingdom, there is no strong historical evidence to suggest that Buddha spent a significant amount of time or had a direct and extensive association with Avanti during his lifetime and preaching period. His activities were concentrated primarily in the Gangetic plains. 2. Gandhara: Gandhara was located in the present-day Afghanistan and Pakistan region. Although it later became a crucial center for Buddhist art and learning (Gandhara school of art), it was geographically distant and not directly associated with the life and early teachings of Gautama Buddha. Buddha's early life and enlightenment were centered in the Gangetic plain. 3. Kosala: This statement is correct. Buddha spent a considerable part of his monastic life, particularly many rainy seasons, in Kosala. Its capital, Sravasti (Sahet-Mahet), was a frequent residence of Buddha, where he delivered numerous discourses and established important monasteries like Jetavana. 4. Magadha: This statement is correct. Magadha was the kingdom where many pivotal events of Buddha's life occurred. He attained enlightenment in Bodh Gaya (then part of Magadha), delivered his first sermon in Sarnath (near Varanasi, which was influenced by Magadha), and spent extensive periods teaching in its cities like Rajagriha (the capital) and Nalanda. Many of his significant disciples also hailed from Magadha. Therefore, only Kosala and Magadha are directly and significantly associated with the life of the Buddha.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 3 and 4 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: 1, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: 3 and 4 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **kingdoms were associated with the life of the buddha?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following is associated with the issue of control and phasing out of the use of ozone-depleting substances?

(a) Bretton Woods Conference  
(b) Montreal Protocol  
(c) Kyoto Protocol  
(d) Nagoya Protocol  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Montreal Protocol**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Montreal Protocol on Substances that Deplete the Ozone Layer is an international treaty designed to protect the ozone layer by phasing out the production and consumption of numerous substances that are responsible for ozone depletion. It was agreed upon in 1987 and entered into force in 1989. It is widely considered one of the most successful international environmental agreements. The Bretton Woods Conference (1944) led to the establishment of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and the World Bank. The Kyoto Protocol (1997) is an international treaty aimed at reducing greenhouse gas emissions to combat climate change. The Nagoya Protocol (2010) is a supplementary agreement to the Convention on Biological Diversity, focused on access to genetic resources and the fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from their utilization. Therefore, the Montreal Protocol is the correct answer.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Montreal Protocol. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: Montreal Protocol** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Kyoto Protocol** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Nagoya Protocol** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **is associated with the issue of control and phasing out of the use of ozone-depleting...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. ## Q41. Consider the following
**Primary route:** Medieval History → [Mughal Society, Economy and Culture](../../topics/medieval-history/mughal-society-economy-and-culture.md) → Consider the following  
**Primary classification:** Medieval History → Mughal Society, Economy and Culture → Consider the following  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q41 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Medieval-Indian-History/basic/12_Babur-and-Central-Asian-Backdrop.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Medieval-Indian-History/basic/12_Babur-and-Central-Asian-Backdrop.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `medieval-history-consider-the-following`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following:  
The arrival of Babur into India led to the  
1. introduction of gunpowder in the subcontinent  
2. introduction of the arch and dome in the region's architecture  
3. establishment of Timurid dynasty in the region  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding Babur's arrival in India: 1. introduction of gunpowder in the subcontinent: This statement is incorrect. Gunpowder was present and used in India before Babur's arrival. For instance, the Bahmani Kingdom and other regional powers had employed gunpowder and firearms. However, Babur's effective and strategic use of artillery (cannons) and gunpowder in battles like Panipat certainly revolutionized warfare in India, but he did not 'introduce' it. 2. introduction of the arch and dome in the region's architecture: This statement is incorrect. The architectural styles incorporating arches and domes were introduced to India much earlier with the advent of Islamic rule during the Delhi Sultanate period (e.g., Qutub Minar complex, Alai Darwaza). These features were prevalent long before the Mughals. 3. establishment of Timurid dynasty in the region: This statement is correct. Babur was a direct descendant of Timur (Tamerlane) on his father's side. When he established his rule in India by defeating Ibrahim Lodi at the Battle of Panipat in 1526, he founded the Mughal Empire, which was essentially a branch of the Timurid dynasty in India. Therefore, only statement 3 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **consider the following**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Government of India established NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India) on January 1, 2015, through a resolution of the Union Cabinet. NITI Aayog was set up to replace the 65-year-old Planning Commission, which had been in existence since 1950. The new institution was envisioned as a premier policy 'think-tank' of the Government of India, providing both directional and policy inputs, in contrast to the Planning Commission's role in centralized planning and allocation.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is Rio+20 Conference, often mentioned in the news?

(a) It is the United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development  
(b) It is a Ministerial Meeting of the World Trade Organization  
(c) It is a Conference of the Inter-governmental Panel on Climate Change  
(d) It is a Conference of the Member Countries of the Convention on Biological Diversity  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — It is the United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Rio+20 Conference, often mentioned in the news, is officially known as the United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development (UNCSD). It was held in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, in June 2012, twenty years after the landmark 1992 Earth Summit (also known as the UN Conference on Environment and Development or UNCED) held in the same city. The main objectives of Rio+20 were to secure renewed political commitment for sustainable development, assess the progress and implementation gaps concerning the agreed commitments, and address new and emerging challenges.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q044"></a>
## Q44. Consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Polity → [Prime Minister and Council of Ministers](../../topics/polity/prime-minister-and-council-of-ministers.md) → Consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Prime Minister and Council of Ministers → Consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q44 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/PM-and-Council-of-Ministers.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/PM-and-Council-of-Ministers.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements :  
1. The Executive Power of the Union of India is vested in the Prime Minister.  
2. The Prime Minister is the ex officio Chairman of the Civil Services Board.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the given statements: 1. The Executive Power of the Union of India is vested in the Prime Minister: This statement is incorrect. According to Article 53(1) of the Indian Constitution, the executive power of the Union is vested in the President and shall be exercised by him either directly or through officers subordinate to him in accordance with this Constitution. While the Prime Minister is the real executive head and exercises most of the executive powers in practice, the formal and constitutional vesting of executive power is in the President. 2. The Prime Minister is the ex officio Chairman of the Civil Services Board: This statement is incorrect. The Civil Services Board is typically chaired by the Cabinet Secretary, who is the senior-most civil servant in India. The Prime Minister is the head of the government and the political executive, but not the ex-officio chairman of the Civil Services Board. Therefore, neither statement 1 nor statement 2 is correct.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term 'Goldilocks Zone' is often seen in the news in the context of

(a) the limits of habitable zone above the surface of the Earth  
(b) regions inside the Earth where shale gas is available  
(c) search for the Earth-like planets in outer space  
(d) search for meteorites containing precious metals  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — search for the Earth-like planets in outer space**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term 'Goldilocks Zone' is frequently used in astrophysics and exoplanetary science. It refers to the habitable zone around a star where conditions are 'just right' for liquid water to exist on a planet's surface. This means the planet is neither too close to its star (where water would evaporate) nor too far (where water would freeze). Liquid water is considered a fundamental requirement for life as we know it. Therefore, the concept of the Goldilocks Zone is central to the ongoing search for Earth-like planets in outer space that could potentially harbor extraterrestrial life.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — search for the Earth-like planets in outer space. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the limits of habitable zone above the surface of the Earth** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: regions inside the Earth where shale gas is available** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: search for the Earth-like planets in outer space** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: search for meteorites containing precious metals** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **goldilocks zone' is often seen in the news in the context of**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

During the Civil Disobedience Movement in 1930, inspired by Mahatma Gandhi's Dandi March, similar salt satyagrahas were organized across different parts of India. He marched from Tiruchirappalli (Trichy) to Vedaranyam on the Tanjore (Thanjavur) coast in April 1930 to break the Salt Law. This was a significant regional demonstration of the larger national movement.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Who of the following founded a new city on the south bank of a tributary to river Krishna and undertook to rule his new kingdom as the agent of a deity to whom all the land south of the river Krishna was supposed to belong?

(a) Amoghavarsha I  
(b) Ballala II  
(c) Harihara I  
(d) Prataparudra II  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Harihara I**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Harihara I, along with his brother Bukka Raya I, founded the Vijayanagara Empire in 1336 AD. They established their capital on the southern bank of the Tungabhadra River, which is a major tributary of the Krishna River, in what is present-day Hampi. The rulers of Vijayanagara, particularly the early ones, considered themselves as the agents or representatives of Lord Virupaksha (a form of Shiva), the presiding deity of the Virupaksha Temple at Hampi. They believed that all the land south of the Krishna River belonged to this deity, and they were ruling on his behalf, providing a strong religious legitimacy to their empire.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — Harihara I. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **C: Harihara I** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

## Q48. First woman and first Muslim presidents of the Indian National Congress
**Primary route:** Modern History → [National Movement Organisations and Leaders](../../topics/modern-history/national-movement-organisations-and-leaders.md) → First woman and first Muslim presidents of the Indian National Congress  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → National Movement Organisations and Leaders → First woman and first Muslim presidents of the Indian National Congress

**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/14_Foundation-of-INC-and-Moderate-Phase.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/14_Foundation-of-INC-and-Moderate-Phase.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-first-woman-and-first-muslim-presidents-of-the-indian-national-congress`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:  
1. The first woman President of the Indian National Congress was Sarojini Naidu.  
2. The first Muslim President of the Indian National Congress was Badruddin Tyabji.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding the Presidents of the Indian National Congress: 1. The first woman President of the Indian National Congress was Sarojini Naidu: This statement is incorrect. The first woman President of the Indian National Congress was Annie Besant, who presided over the Calcutta Session in 1917. Sarojini Naidu was the first Indian woman President of the Congress, presiding over the Kanpur Session in 1925. 2. The first Muslim President of the Indian National Congress was Badruddin Tyabji: This statement is correct. Badruddin Tyabji presided over the third session of the Indian National Congress, held in Madras (Chennai) in 1887, becoming the first Muslim to hold this prestigious position. Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

## Q49. Statements regarding 'Green Climate Fund' is/are correct?
**Primary route:** Environment → [UNFCCC, COP, Kyoto and Paris Agreement](../../topics/environment/unfccc-cop-kyoto-paris-agreement.md) → Statements regarding 'Green Climate Fund' is/are correct?

**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/19_UNFCCC-COP-Kyoto-Paris-Agreement.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/19_UNFCCC-COP-Kyoto-Paris-Agreement.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-statements-regarding-green-climate-fund-is-are-correct`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements regarding 'Green Climate Fund' is/are correct?  
1. It is intended to assist the developing countries in adaptation and mitigation practices to counter climate change.  
2. It is founded under the aegis of UNEP, OECD, Asian Development Bank and World Bank.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding the 'Green Climate Fund' (GCF): 1. It is intended to assist the developing countries in adaptation and mitigation practices to counter climate change: This statement is correct. The Green Climate Fund was established to support developing countries' efforts to address climate change. Its mandate is to finance adaptation and mitigation projects, programs, policies, and other activities in these nations. 2. It is founded under the aegis of UNEP, OECD, Asian Development Bank and World Bank: This statement is incorrect. The Green Climate Fund was established by 194 countries at the 2010 United Nations Climate Change Conference (COP 16) in Cancun, Mexico, as an operating entity of the financial mechanism of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). While organizations like UNEP, the World Bank, and regional development banks might be involved in various capacities (e.g., as implementing agencies or providers of expertise), they are not the founding bodies or direct aegis under which the GCF was established. Its foundation is rooted directly in the UNFCCC process. Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **statements regarding 'green climate fund' is/are correct?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q51 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/26_Post-War-Upsurge-INA-RIN-Mutiny-Cabinet-Mission.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/26_Post-War-Upsurge-INA-RIN-Mutiny-Cabinet-Mission.md)

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Cabinet Mission, which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It recommended a federal government.  
2. It enlarged the powers of the Indian courts.  
3. It provided for more Indians in the ICS.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Cabinet Mission Plan, proposed in 1946, primarily aimed at devising a framework for India's independence and the formation of a Constituent Assembly. One of its key recommendations was the establishment of a Union of India, which would comprise both British India and the princely states. This Union was to have powers over foreign affairs, defence, and communications, while all residuary powers would vest in the provinces. This structure, with a division of powers between the centre and the provinces/states, clearly indicates a recommendation for a federal government. Therefore, statement 1 is correct. The Mission's proposals did not directly address the enlargement of powers of Indian courts (statement 2) or the provision for more Indians in the Indian Civil Service (ICS) (statement 3). Its focus was on the constitutional machinery for the transfer of power, not the specifics of judicial or administrative reforms in that manner.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: None** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following National Parks has a climate that varies from tropical to subtropical, temperate and arctic?

(a) Khangchendzonga National Park  
(b) Nandadevi National Park  
(c) Neora Valley National Park  
(d) Namdapha National Park  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Namdapha National Park**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Namdapha National Park, located in Arunachal Pradesh, India, is renowned for its exceptional biodiversity and unique altitudinal variations. Its elevation ranges from 200 meters to over 4,500 meters above sea level. This vast altitudinal gradient results in a climatic spectrum that astonishingly varies from tropical (at lower altitudes) to subtropical, temperate, and even alpine/arctic conditions (at higher altitudes). This unique characteristic allows it to support an incredible diversity of flora and fauna, making it a hotspot of biodiversity. While other national parks listed (Khangchendzonga, Nandadevi, Neora Valley) are also in mountainous regions and exhibit varied climates, Namdapha is particularly known for encompassing such a broad range from tropical to arctic within its boundaries due to its specific geographical location and extreme elevation differences.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Namdapha National Park. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **C: Neora Valley National Park** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Namdapha National Park** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

(a) an agency of the United Nations to help refugees of civil wars

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Amnesty International is a widely recognized non-governmental organization that operates globally. Its primary mission is to campaign for human rights. It focuses on preventing and ending grave abuses of human rights and demanding justice for those whose rights have been violated. The organization conducts research, advocacy, and campaigns to promote all human rights enshrined in the Universal Declaration of Human Rights and other international standards. It is not an agency of the United Nations (A), nor is its primary focus limited to helping refugees of civil wars or very poor people (C). While it may assist in such situations as part of its human rights mandate, its scope is broader. It is also not an inter-governmental agency (D) but rather an independent NGO.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the art and archaeological history of India, which one among the following was made earliest?

(a) Lingaraja Temple at Bhubaneswar  
(b) Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli  
(c) Rock-cut Monuments at Mahabalipuram  
(d) Varaha Image at Udayagiri  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

To determine which of the given architectural/artistic works was made earliest, one needs to know their approximate periods of creation: Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli: This famous elephant sculpture, located in Odisha, is associated with the Ashokan rock edicts and dates back to the Mauryan period, specifically the 3rd century BCE. It is considered one of the earliest rock-cut sculptures in India. Varaha Image at Udayagiri: The Udayagiri Caves in Madhya Pradesh, including the magnificent Varaha (boar) avatar of Vishnu, belong to the Gupta period, dating to the early 5th century CE. Rock-cut Monuments at Mahabalipuram: These include the Rathas and mandapas, created by the Pallava dynasty in Tamil Nadu, primarily during the 7th and 8th centuries CE. Lingaraja Temple at Bhubaneswar: This grand temple in Odisha is a classic example of Kalinga architecture and was largely completed in the 11th century CE. Comparing these periods, the Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli (3rd century BCE) predates all the others by several centuries, making it the earliest.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: Rock-cut Elephant at Dhauli** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Rock-cut Monuments at Mahabalipuram** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Varaha Image at Udayagiri** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Indian history, which of the following is/are the essential element/elements of the feudal system?  
1. A very strong centralized political authority and a very weak provincial or local political authority  
2. Emergence of administrative structure based on control and possession of land  
3. Creation of lord-vassal relationship between the feudal lord and his overlord  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The feudal system, both in India and globally, is characterized by specific socio-economic and political arrangements: Statement 1: A very strong centralized political authority and a very weak provincial or local political authority. This is incorrect. Feudalism is typically characterized by a decentralized political authority, where the power of the central ruler is weak, and significant power rests with powerful local lords (feudatories or samantas) who control their territories. Statement 2: Emergence of administrative structure based on control and possession of land. This is correct. Land was the primary source of wealth and power in a feudal system. Kings granted land to their vassals (feudal lords) in exchange for military service and loyalty. These lords, in turn, controlled the land and administered justice within their domains. Statement 3: Creation of lord-vassal relationship between the feudal lord and his overlord. This is correct. A hierarchical structure, where a lesser lord (vassal) owed loyalty and service (often military) to a greater lord (overlord or king) in exchange for land or protection, is a defining characteristic of feudalism. This relationship was based on mutual obligations. Therefore, statements 2 and 3 correctly describe essential elements of the feudal system in Indian history.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **indian history, which of the following is/are the essential element/elements of the...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

While India has expressed interest in full membership, it has not yet been granted. 2. Association of South-East Asian Nations (ASEAN): India is a dialogue partner of ASEAN, but not a full member. ASEAN's membership is limited to Southeast Asian countries. 3. East Asia Summit (EAS): India is one of the 18 member countries of the East Asia Summit. The EAS is a premier forum for strategic dialogue and cooperation on political, security, and economic issues of common regional concern. Therefore, among the given options, India is only a member of the East Asia Summit.

## Q58. In India, the steel production industry requires the import of
**Primary route:** Geography → [Mineral and Energy Resources: World and India](../../topics/geography/mineral-energy-resources-world-and-india.md) → In India, the steel production industry requires the import of  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Mineral and Energy Resources: World and India → In India, the steel production industry requires the import of  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q58 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-in-india-the-steel-production-industry-requires-the-import-of`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In India, the steel production industry requires the import of

(a) saltpetre  
(b) rock phosphate  
(c) coking coal  
(d) All of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — coking coal**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

India is a major producer of iron ore, which is the primary raw material for steel production. However, despite having significant coal reserves, India faces a shortage of high-quality coking coal, which is essential for the metallurgical process (blast furnace) involved in steel manufacturing. Coking coal, when heated in the absence of air, produces coke, which acts as a fuel and a reducing agent in the blast furnace to convert iron ore into pig iron. India's domestic coking coal reserves are limited and often of inferior quality, necessitating substantial imports to meet the demands of its steel industry. Saltpetre (potassium nitrate) is used in fertilizers and explosives, and rock phosphate is used in fertilizers; neither is a primary import for the steel industry in the same critical capacity as coking coal.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — coking coal. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: saltpetre** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: rock phosphate** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: coking coal** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: All of the above** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **in india, the steel production industry requires the import of**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

**Canonical slug:** `polity-provisions-in-fifth-schedule-and-sixth-schedule-in-the-constitution-of-india-are-made-in`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The provisions in Fifth Schedule and Sixth Schedule in the Constitution of India are made in order to

(a) protect the interests of Scheduled Tribes  
(b) determine the boundaries between States  
(c) determine the powers, authority and responsibilities of Panchayats  
(d) protect the interests of all the border States  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — protect the interests of Scheduled Tribes**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Fifth Schedule and Sixth Schedule of the Constitution of India are special provisions designed to protect the interests and cultural autonomy of Scheduled Tribes and to ensure their effective administration. Fifth Schedule: This schedule deals with the administration and control of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes in states other than Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, and Mizoram. It empowers the President to declare any area as a Scheduled Area and establishes Tribal Advisory Councils to advise on welfare and advancement of STs. Sixth Schedule: This schedule contains special provisions for the administration of tribal areas in the four northeastern states of Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, and Mizoram. It provides for the creation of Autonomous District Councils (ADCs) and Regional Councils, which have powers to legislate on various matters including land, forest, shifting cultivation, administration of villages, inheritance of property, marriage and divorce, social customs, etc., thereby granting a significant degree of autonomy to tribal communities. Both schedules are fundamentally aimed at safeguarding the rights, culture, and interests of Scheduled Tribes and ensuring their development while preserving their distinct identity. They do not pertain to determining state boundaries, powers of Panchayats (which are addressed by the 73rd and 74th Amendments, not these schedules), or the interests of all border states broadly.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — protect the interests of Scheduled Tribes. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: protect the interests of Scheduled Tribes** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: determine the boundaries between States** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: determine the powers, authority and responsibilities of Panchayats** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: protect the interests of all the border States** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **provisions in fifth schedule and sixth schedule in the constitution of india are made in...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q060"></a>
## Q60. The Union Government, consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Economy → [Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators](../../topics/economy/union-budget-fiscal-policy-and-deficit-indicators.md) → The Union Government, consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators → The Union Government, consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q60 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-the-union-government-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Union Government, consider the following statements:  
1. The Department of Revenue is responsible for the preparation of Union Budget that is presented to the Parliament.  
2. No amount can be withdrawn from the Consolidated Fund of India without the authorization from the Parliament of India.  
3. All the disbursements made from Public Account also need the authorization from the Parliament of India.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 2 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze each statement regarding the Union Government's financial procedures: 1. The Department of Revenue is responsible for the preparation of Union Budget that is presented to the Parliament. This statement is incorrect. The Union Budget is prepared by the Budget Division of the Department of Economic Affairs, which is part of the Ministry of Finance, not the Department of Revenue. 2. No amount can be withdrawn from the Consolidated Fund of India without the authorization from the Parliament of India. This statement is correct. Article 266(3) of the Constitution clearly states that "No money out of the Consolidated Fund of India or the Consolidated Fund of a State shall be appropriated except in accordance with law and for the purposes and in the manner provided in this Constitution." This means parliamentary authorization (through an Appropriation Act) is mandatory for any withdrawal from the Consolidated Fund. 3. All the disbursements made from Public Account also need the authorization from the Parliament of India. This statement is incorrect. The Public Account of India (Article 266(2)) includes funds like provident funds, small savings, and remittances, where the government acts as a banker. Money in the Public Account does not belong to the government but is held by it on behalf of others. Disbursements from the Public Account do not require parliamentary authorization because they are not money appropriated from the Consolidated Fund; they are merely transactions of funds already held. Based on the analysis, only statement 2 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **the union government, consider the following statements**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q061"></a>
## Q61. Who/Which of the following is the custodian of the Constitution of India?
**Primary route:** Polity → [Supreme Court](../../topics/polity/supreme-court.md) → Who/Which of the following is the custodian of the Constitution of India?  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Supreme Court → Who/Which of the following is the custodian of the Constitution of India?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q61 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Supreme-Court.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Supreme-Court.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-who-which-of-the-following-is-the-custodian-of-the-constitution-of-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Who/Which of the following is the custodian of the Constitution of India?

(a) The President of India  
(b) The Prime Minister of India  
(c) The Lok Sabha Secretariat  
(d) The Supreme Court of India  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — The Supreme Court of India**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Supreme Court of India acts as the final interpreter and guardian of the Constitution. Its role as the 'custodian' or 'guardian' of the Constitution implies several key functions: Judicial Review: The Supreme Court has the power of judicial review, allowing it to examine the constitutionality of legislative enactments and executive orders of both the Centre and the states. If a law or order violates the Constitution, it can be declared null and void by the Supreme Court. Protector of Fundamental Rights: It safeguards the fundamental rights of citizens, acting as the guarantor of these rights as enshrined in the Constitution. Upholding Constitutional Supremacy: By ensuring that all laws and actions of the government adhere to the provisions of the Constitution, the Supreme Court maintains the supremacy of the Constitution. Arbitration of Disputes: It resolves disputes between the Centre and the states, or between states, concerning constitutional matters. While the President is the head of state and upholds the Constitution, and Parliament makes laws, it is the Supreme Court that ultimately interprets and protects the Constitution from any encroachment or violation.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — The Supreme Court of India. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The President of India** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: The Prime Minister of India** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: The Lok Sabha Secretariat** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: The Supreme Court of India** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **who/which of the following is the custodian of the constitution of india?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

## Q62. Consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Economy → [Irrigation, Inputs, Credit, Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture](../../topics/economy/irrigation-inputs-credit-insurance-and-sustainable-agriculture.md) → Consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Irrigation, Inputs, Credit, Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture → Consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q62 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements :  
1. The Accelerated Irrigation Benefits Programme was launched during 1996-97 to provide loan assistance to poor farmers.  
2. The Command Area Development Programme was launched in 1974-75 for the development of water-use efficiency.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate the given statements about irrigation programmes in India: 1. The Accelerated Irrigation Benefits Programme (AIBP) was launched during 1996-97 to provide loan assistance to poor farmers. This statement is incorrect. The AIBP, launched in 1996-97, was designed to provide central assistance to states for the early completion of ongoing major and medium irrigation projects, as well as surface minor irrigation schemes. Its primary objective was to expedite the creation of irrigation potential, not to provide direct loan assistance to individual farmers. 2. The Command Area Development Programme (CADP) was launched in 1974-75 for the development of water-use efficiency. This statement is correct. The CADP (later restructured as the Command Area Development and Water Management Programme or CADWM) was indeed launched in 1974-75 with the aim of improving the utilization of created irrigation potential and optimizing water use efficiency in irrigation projects. This included activities like on-farm development works, construction of field channels, and promotion of efficient water management practices. Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **consider the following statements**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Genetic Engineering Appraisal Committee is constituted under the

(a) Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006  
(b) Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999  
(c) Environment (Protection) Act, 1986  
(d) Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Environment (Protection) Act, 1986**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Genetic Engineering Appraisal Committee (GEAC) is constituted under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. Specifically, it was established under the Rules for the Manufacture, Use/Import/Export and Storage of Hazardous Microorganisms/Genetically Engineered Organisms or Cells, 1989, which were framed under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. GEAC functions under the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change and is responsible for approving activities involving large-scale use of hazardous microorganisms and genetically engineered organisms.

In the Mekong-Ganga Cooperation, an initiative of six countries, which of the following is/are not a participant/participants?

Cambodia: Is a participant in MGC. 3. China: Not a participant in MGC. (Although the Mekong River originates in China, China is not part of this specific cooperation initiative.) 4. Myanmar: Is a participant in MGC. 5. Thailand: Is a participant in MGC. Therefore, Bangladesh (1) and China (3) are not participants in the Mekong-Ganga Cooperation.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

'Basel III Accord' or simply 'Basel III', often seen in the news, seeks to

(a) develop national strategies for the conservation and sustainable use of biological diversity  
(b) improve banking sector's ability to deal with financial and economic stress and improve risk management  
(c) reduce the greenhouse gas emissions but places a heavier burden on developed countries  
(d) transfer technology from developed countries to poor countries to enable them to replace the use of chlorofluorocarbons in refrigeration with harmless chemicals  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — improve banking sector's ability to deal with financial and economic stress and improve risk management**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

'Basel III' is an internationally agreed set of measures developed by the Basel Committee on Banking Supervision in response to the global financial crisis of 2007-09. Its primary objective is to strengthen the regulation, supervision, and risk management within the banking sector. The Accord aims to improve the banking sector's ability to absorb shocks arising from financial and economic stress, reduce the risk of spillover from the financial sector to the real economy, and improve risk management and governance. It does this by increasing bank capital requirements, introducing new liquidity standards, and measures to curb excessive leverage. Options A, C, and D describe other international agreements or goals (biodiversity conservation, greenhouse gas emissions reduction, and ozone-depleting substances replacement), which are unrelated to the Basel Accords' focus on banking regulation.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — improve banking sector's ability to deal with financial and economic stress and improve risk management. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: develop national strategies for the conservation and sustainable use of biological diversity** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: improve banking sector's ability to deal with financial and economic stress and improve risk management** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: reduce the greenhouse gas emissions but places a heavier burden on developed countries** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: transfer technology from developed countries to poor countries to enable them to replace the use of chlorofluorocarbons in refrigeration with harmless chemicals** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **basel iii accord' or simply 'basel iii', often seen in the news, seeks to**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact.
