**Canonical slug:** `geography-westerlies-and-north-west-india-s-winter-rainfall`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:  
1. The winds which blow between 30° N and 60° S latitudes through-out the year are known as westerlies.  
2. The moist air masses that cause winter rains in North-Western region of India are part of westerlies.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding winds and winter rains: 1. The winds which blow between 30° N and 60° S latitudes throughout the year are known as westerlies. This statement is incorrect. Westerlies are global winds that blow predominantly from the west towards the east in the middle latitudes (between approximately 30° and 60° latitude) in *both* the Northern and Southern Hemispheres. The statement incorrectly combines latitude ranges from different hemispheres (30° N and 60° S) and doesn't accurately describe the typical latitudinal band for westerlies in both hemispheres. 2. The moist air masses that cause winter rains in North-Western region of India are part of westerlies. This statement is correct. The winter rainfall in the North-Western plains of India (e.g., Punjab, Haryana, parts of Uttar Pradesh) is caused by Western Disturbances. These are extra-tropical storm systems that originate in the Mediterranean region and travel eastward, carried by the subtropical westerly jet stream. These disturbances bring moisture and cause precipitation as they interact with the Himalayas. Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **westerlies and north-west india's winter rainfall**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Indian Ocean Rim Association for Regional Cooperation (IOR-ARC)', consider the following statements:  
1. It was established very recently in response to incidents of piracy and accidents of oil spills.  
2. It is an alliance meant for maritime security only.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate the statements regarding the Indian Ocean Rim Association for Regional Cooperation (IOR-ARC), now known as the Indian Ocean Rim Association (IORA): 1. It was established very recently in response to incidents of piracy and accidents of oil spills. This statement is incorrect. IORA was established in March 1997, which is not 'very recently' from a historical perspective. While maritime security, including issues like piracy and oil spills, is one of its areas of focus, the association was formed with broader objectives of promoting regional cooperation and sustained growth and balanced development within the Indian Ocean region. Its establishment was not solely or primarily in response to these specific incidents. 2. It is an alliance meant for maritime security only. This statement is incorrect. While maritime safety and security is a crucial area of cooperation for IORA, its mandate is much broader. Its key priority areas include trade and investment facilitation, fisheries management, disaster risk management, academic and science & technology cooperation, tourism and cultural exchanges, and blue economy, in addition to maritime safety and security. Therefore, it is not 'only' for maritime security. Since both statements are incorrect, the correct answer is 'Neither 1 nor 2'.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **indian ocean rim association for regional cooperation (ior-arc)', consider the following...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. ### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following movements has contributed to a split in the Indian National Congress resulting in the emergence of 'moderates' and 'extremists?

(a) Swadeshi Movement  
(b) Quit India Movement  
(c) Non-Cooperation Movement  
(d) Civil Disobedience Movement  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Swadeshi Movement**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The split in the Indian National Congress into 'Moderates' and 'Extremists' culminated at the Surat Session in 1907. This ideological divergence had been brewing for some time, but it became particularly pronounced during and after the Swadeshi Movement (1905-1908). The Swadeshi Movement, launched in response to the Partition of Bengal in 1905, saw differing approaches between the Moderates and Extremists. The Moderates preferred constitutional methods and petitions, limiting the movement to Bengal, while the Extremists advocated for more aggressive methods like boycott, passive resistance, and extending the movement beyond Bengal. The differing views on the goals, methods, and leadership of the Swadeshi Movement exacerbated the existing fissures between the two factions, leading to the open split at Surat in 1907. The other movements (Quit India Movement, Non-Cooperation Movement, Civil Disobedience Movement) occurred much later and involved different phases of the nationalist struggle, with the Congress largely united under Mahatma Gandhi's leadership, though internal differences always existed.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — Swadeshi Movement. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: Quit India Movement** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: Non-Cooperation Movement** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: Civil Disobedience Movement** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **movements has contributed to a split in the indian national congress resulting in the...**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In a particular region in India, the local people train the roots of living trees into robust bridges across the streams. As the time passes, these bridges become stronger. These unique 'living root bridges' are found in

(a) Meghalaya  
(b) Himachal Pradesh  
(c) Jharkhand  
(d) Tamil Nadu  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Meghalaya**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The unique 'living root bridges' (locally known as 'Jing Kieng Jri') are an indigenous engineering marvel primarily found in the state of Meghalaya, in Northeast India. The local Khasi and Jaintia tribes have, for centuries, trained the aerial roots of the Ficus elastica (Indian rubber tree) across streams and rivers. Over time, these roots intertwine and grow stronger, forming sturdy, living bridges that can last for hundreds of years. This sustainable form of bioengineering is a testament to the symbiotic relationship between the local communities and their environment in Meghalaya's moist, tropical climate which is ideal for root growth.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q070"></a>
## Q70. Tides occur in the oceans and seas due to which among the following?
**Primary route:** Geography → [Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity](../../topics/geography/oceans-currents-tides-and-salinity.md) → Tides occur in the oceans and seas due to which among the following?  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Oceans, Currents, Tides and Salinity → Tides occur in the oceans and seas due to which among the following?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q70 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/12_The-Oceans-Currents-Tides-Salinity.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/12_The-Oceans-Currents-Tides-Salinity.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-tides-occur-in-the-oceans-and-seas-due-to-which-among-the-following`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Tides occur in the oceans and seas due to which among the following?  
1. Gravitational force of the Sun  
2. Gravitational force of the Moon  
3. Centrifugal force of the Earth  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Tides are the rise and fall of sea levels caused by the combined effects of the gravitational forces exerted by the Moon and the Sun, and the rotation of the Earth. All three factors contribute to the phenomenon of tides: 1. Gravitational force of the Sun: The Sun's gravitational pull, though weaker than the Moon's due to its greater distance, still exerts a significant influence on Earth's tides. When the Sun, Moon, and Earth align (during new moon and full moon), their combined gravitational pull creates stronger 'spring tides'. When they are at right angles (during quarter moons), they create weaker 'neap tides'. 2. Gravitational force of the Moon: The Moon is the primary cause of tides. Its gravitational pull creates a bulge of water on the side of Earth closest to it. On the opposite side, a similar bulge occurs because the Earth itself is pulled towards the Moon more strongly than the water on the far side, leaving the water behind. 3. Centrifugal force of the Earth: As the Earth and Moon revolve around their common center of mass, a centrifugal force is generated. This force pushes water outwards from the center of rotation. On the side of the Earth opposite to the Moon, this centrifugal force, combined with the weaker gravitational pull of the Moon, contributes to the formation of the tidal bulge. Therefore, all three forces collectively cause the tides in oceans and seas.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — 1, 2 and 3. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **tides occur in the oceans and seas due to which among the following?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q71. In which of the following activities are Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites used?
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Satellites, NavIC, GAGAN and Applications](../../topics/science-and-technology/satellites-navic-gagan-and-applications.md) → In which of the following activities are Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites used?  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Satellites, NavIC, GAGAN and Applications → In which of the following activities are Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites used?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q71 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/02_Satellites-NavIC-GAGAN-and-Applications.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/02_Satellites-NavIC-GAGAN-and-Applications.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-in-which-of-the-following-activities-are-indian-remote-sensing-irs-satellites-used`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In which of the following activities are Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites used?  
1. Assessment of crop productivity  
2. Locating groundwater resources  
3. Mineral exploration  
4. Telecommunications  
5. Traffic studies  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3 only  
(b) 4 and 5 only  
(c) 1 and 2 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1, 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites are primarily designed and used for Earth observation, resource management, and environmental monitoring. Their applications include: 1. Assessment of crop productivity: Remote sensing data is extensively used in agriculture for crop mapping, yield estimation, monitoring crop health, and assessing drought conditions. This makes statement 1 correct. 2. Locating groundwater resources: Satellite imagery helps in identifying geological structures, landforms, and vegetation patterns that indicate the presence of groundwater potential. This makes statement 2 correct. 3. Mineral exploration: Remote sensing techniques assist in geological mapping, identifying mineral-rich areas, and structural features associated with mineral deposits. This makes statement 3 correct. 4. Telecommunications: This is the primary function of communication satellites (like India's INSAT series), not remote sensing satellites. Remote sensing satellites capture data about the Earth's surface, they do not facilitate telephone, internet, or television communication directly. This makes statement 4 incorrect. 5. Traffic studies: While high-resolution satellite imagery can potentially be used for urban planning and traffic monitoring, it's not a primary or core application commonly associated with the broader functions of IRS satellites compared to the first three. However, the most definitive incorrect option is telecommunications. Given the options, 1, 2, and 3 are the most direct and well-established applications. Thus, the correct combination is 1, 2, and 3 only.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1, 2 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **B: 4 and 5 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **in which of the following activities are indian remote sensing (irs) satellites used?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q72. Consider the following States
**Primary route:** Environment → [Forest Types and Forest Rights Act](../../topics/environment/forest-types-and-forest-rights-act.md) → Consider the following States  
**Primary classification:** Environment → Forest Types and Forest Rights Act → Consider the following States

**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-consider-the-following-states`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following States:  
1. Arunachal Pradesh  
2. Himachal Pradesh  
3. Mizoram  
In which of the above States do 'Tropical Wet Evergreen Forests' occur?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Tropical Wet Evergreen Forests are characterized by high rainfall (typically over 200 cm annually) and high temperatures throughout the year, leading to dense, multi-layered vegetation with evergreen trees. They are found in regions close to the equator or in areas with persistent monsoonal rainfall. 1. Arunachal Pradesh: The lower altitudes and eastern parts of Arunachal Pradesh, especially the Mishmi Hills and Patkai ranges, receive abundant rainfall and experience warm temperatures, supporting extensive tropical wet evergreen forests. 2. Himachal Pradesh: Located in the Western Himalayas, Himachal Pradesh primarily experiences temperate, sub-alpine, and alpine climates. Its forests are mainly temperate coniferous, sub-tropical broad-leaved, or dry deciduous, but not tropical wet evergreen. 3. Mizoram: As part of the Northeast India region, Mizoram receives very heavy rainfall (often exceeding 250 cm annually) and has a warm, humid climate ideal for the growth of tropical wet evergreen forests. These forests are a dominant vegetation type in the state. Therefore, tropical wet evergreen forests occur in Arunachal Pradesh and Mizoram, but not in Himachal Pradesh.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 3 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term 'IndARC', sometimes seen in the news, is the name of

(a) an indigenously developed radar system inducted into Indian Defence  
(b) India's satellite to provide services to the countries of Indian Ocean Rim  
(c) a scientific establishment set up by India in Antarctic region  
(d) India's underwater observatory to scientifically study the Arctic region  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — India's underwater observatory to scientifically study the Arctic region**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

'IndARC' is the name of India's first multi-sensor moored observatory in the Arctic. It was deployed in 2014 at a depth of 192 meters in the Kongsfjorden fjord, Svalbard, Norway, an Arctic archipelago. The observatory is designed to collect data on oceanographic parameters like temperature, salinity, currents, and ocean acidification, which are crucial for understanding the impact of climate change in the Arctic region. It is a significant step in India's polar research initiatives, expanding its scientific presence beyond Antarctica to the Arctic. Options A, B, and C describe other types of scientific or defense systems which are not related to 'IndARC'.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — India's underwater observatory to scientifically study the Arctic region. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: an indigenously developed radar system inducted into Indian Defence** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: India's satellite to provide services to the countries of Indian Ocean Rim** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: a scientific establishment set up by India in Antarctic region** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: India's underwater observatory to scientifically study the Arctic region** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Forest Carbon Partnership Facility', which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It is a global partnership of governments, businesses, civil society and indigenous peoples.  
2. It provides financial aid to universities, individual scientists and institutions involved in scientific forestry research to develop eco-friendly and climate adaptation technologies for sustainable forest management.  
3. It assists the countries in their 'REDD+ (Reducing Emissions from Deforestation and Forest Degradation+)' efforts by providing them with financial and technical assistance.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. The Forest Carbon Partnership Facility (FCPF) is a global partnership of governments, businesses, civil society, and indigenous peoples focused on reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation (REDD+) in developing countries. Statement 2 is incorrect. FCPF provides financial and technical assistance to developing countries for their REDD+ readiness and implementation efforts. It does not directly provide financial assistance to universities, individual scientists, or institutions for scientific forestry research. Statement 3 is correct. FCPF assists countries in their REDD+ efforts by providing resources for capacity building, policy development, and establishing reference emission levels. Therefore, only statements 1 and 3 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **forest carbon partnership facility', which of the following statements is/are correct?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. ### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to an organization known as 'BirdLife International', which of the following statements is/are correct?  
1. It is a Global Partnership of Conservation Organizations.  
2. The concept of 'biodiversity hotspots' originated from this organization.  
3. It identifies the sites known/referred to as 'Important Bird and Biodiversity Areas'.  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: BirdLife International is indeed a global partnership of conservation organizations. It is the world's largest partnership of conservation organizations, striving to conserve birds, their habitats, and global biodiversity. Statement 2 is incorrect: The concept of 'biodiversity hotspots' was originated by Norman Myers, a British environmental scientist, in 1988, not by BirdLife International. While BirdLife International works extensively in and identifies key biodiversity areas, they did not conceptualize the term 'biodiversity hotspots'. Statement 3 is correct: BirdLife International is the globally recognized authority for identifying, documenting, and designating 'Important Bird and Biodiversity Areas' (IBAs). IBAs are sites critical for the conservation of bird populations around the world. Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.

- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Jordan**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's examine the geographical location of each country: Syria: Syria has a western coastline along the Mediterranean Sea. Jordan: Jordan is largely landlocked, with its only maritime access being a small stretch of coastline on the Gulf of Aqaba, which is an inlet of the Red Sea, not the Mediterranean Sea. Lebanon: Lebanon lies entirely on the eastern coast of the Mediterranean Sea. Israel: Israel has a significant coastline along the southeastern shore of the Mediterranean Sea. Therefore, Jordan is the country among the given options that does not open out to the Mediterranean Sea.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In India, in which one of the following types of forests is teak a dominant tree species?

(a) Tropical moist deciduous forest  
(b) Tropical rain forest  
(c) Tropical thorn scrub forest  
(d) Temperate forest with grasslands  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Tropical moist deciduous forest**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Teak (Tectona grandis) is a highly valued timber species that is characteristically found in Tropical Moist Deciduous Forests in India. These forests are prevalent in regions receiving rainfall between 100-200 cm annually. They are known as 'monsoon forests' and feature trees that shed their leaves during the dry season to conserve water. Teak thrives in these conditions, making it a dominant species in this forest type. Tropical rainforests are evergreen, while tropical thorn scrub forests are adapted to much drier conditions. Temperate forests are found in cooler climates, which are unsuitable for teak.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — Tropical moist deciduous forest. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **D: Temperate forest with grasslands** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **in india, in which one of the following types of forests is teak a dominant tree species?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. ## Q80. Each day is more or less the same, the morning is clear and bright with a sea breeze; as...
**Primary route:** Geography → [Hot Wet Equatorial Climate](../../topics/geography/hot-wet-equatorial-climate.md) → Each day is more or less the same, the morning is clear and bright with a sea breeze; as...  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Hot Wet Equatorial Climate → Each day is more or less the same, the morning is clear and bright with a sea breeze; as...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q80 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/15_Hot-Wet-Equatorial-Climate.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/15_Hot-Wet-Equatorial-Climate.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-each-day-is-more-or-less-the-same-the-morning-is-clear-and-bright-with-a-sea-breeze-as`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

"Each day is more or less the same, the morning is clear and bright with a sea breeze; as the Sun climbs high in the sky, heat mounts up, dark clouds form, then rain comes with thunder and lightning. But rain is soon over."  
Which of the following regions is described in the above passage?

(a) Savannah  
(b) Equatorial  
(c) Monsoon  
(d) Mediterranean  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Equatorial**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The passage describes a climate characterized by daily uniformity, with mornings starting clear, followed by a build-up of heat, formation of dark clouds, and afternoon rain accompanied by thunder and lightning, which then clears up quickly. This daily pattern of convectional rainfall, high humidity, and consistent high temperatures with little seasonal variation is a defining characteristic of the Equatorial climate region. Regions around the equator (e.g., Amazon Basin, Congo Basin, parts of Southeast Asia) exhibit this weather pattern. Savannah climates have distinct wet and dry seasons. Monsoon climates have seasonal reversal of winds and distinct wet and dry seasons. Mediterranean climates are characterized by dry hot summers and mild, wet winters.

**Statement/item-level result:**

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q081"></a>
## Q81. Indian economy, consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Economy → [National Income, GDP, GVA and Measurement](../../topics/economy/national-income-gdp-gva-and-measurement.md) → Indian economy, consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Economy → National Income, GDP, GVA and Measurement → Indian economy, consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q81 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-indian-economy-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Indian economy, consider the following statements :  
1. The rate of growth of Real Gross Domestic Product has steadily increased in the last decade.  
2. The Gross Domestic Product at market prices (in rupees) has steadily increased in the last decade.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The rate of growth of Real Gross Domestic Product (GDP) in India, or any economy, does not steadily increase over a decade. Economic growth rates are influenced by various domestic and global factors (e.g., economic reforms, global crises, natural disasters, policy changes) and tend to fluctuate significantly year to year. A steady, uninterrupted increase for an entire decade is highly improbable. Statement 2 is correct: The Gross Domestic Product (GDP) at market prices, when expressed in nominal terms (in rupees), generally shows a steady increase over a decade. This is because nominal GDP reflects both real economic growth and inflation. Even if real growth fluctuates, the combined effect of positive real growth and general price increases (inflation) over time almost invariably leads to a steady upward trend in nominal GDP figures. Governments also tend to show an increase in nominal GDP to reflect economic expansion.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

## Q82. State Legislative Council size and election of its Chairman
**Primary route:** Polity → [State Legislatures](../../topics/polity/state-legislatures.md) → State Legislative Council size and election of its Chairman  
**Primary classification:** Polity → State Legislatures → State Legislative Council size and election of its Chairman

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:  
1. The Legislative Council of a State in India can be larger in size than half of the Legislative Assembly of that particular State.  
2. The Governor of a State nominates the Chairman of Legislative Council of that particular State.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: Article 171(1) of the Indian Constitution states that the total number of members in the Legislative Council of a State shall not exceed one-third of the total number of members in the Legislative Assembly of that State. It also stipulates that in no case shall the total number of members in the Legislative Council be less than 40. Therefore, the Legislative Council's size cannot be larger than half of the Legislative Assembly. Statement 2 is incorrect: The Chairman of the Legislative Council is elected by the members of the Legislative Council from amongst themselves, similar to the Speaker of the Legislative Assembly. The Governor does not nominate the Chairman. Since both statements are incorrect, the answer is neither 1 nor 2.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Neither 1 nor 2. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

"To uphold and protect the Sovereignty, Unity and Integrity of India" is a provision made in the

(a) Preamble of the Constitution  
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy  
(c) Fundamental Rights  
(d) Fundamental Duties  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Fundamental Duties**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The phrase "To uphold and protect the Sovereignty, Unity and Integrity of India" is a specific provision enumerated under the Fundamental Duties. Article 51A(c) of the Indian Constitution states that it shall be the duty of every citizen of India "to uphold and protect the sovereignty, unity and integrity of India." While the Preamble reflects these ideals, and they are foundational to the spirit of the Constitution, the direct provision for citizens to uphold and protect them is found in the Fundamental Duties.

**Statement/item-level result:**

- **C: Fundamental Rights** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Fundamental Duties** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following is the best description of the term 'ecosystem?

(a) A community of organisms interacting with one another  
(b) That part of the Earth which is inhabited by living organisms  
(c) A community of organisms together with the environment in which they live  
(d) The flora and fauna of a geographical area  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — A community of organisms together with the environment in which they live**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

An 'ecosystem' is a fundamental concept in ecology. It refers to a functional unit comprising all the living organisms (the biotic community - flora and fauna) in a particular area, interacting with each other, and also interacting with their non-living physical environment (the abiotic components like soil, water, air, sunlight, temperature) as a whole. This interaction creates a flow of energy and cycling of nutrients. Options A, B, and D describe only parts or aspects related to an ecosystem but do not encompass the complete definition that includes both biotic and abiotic interactions.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — A community of organisms together with the environment in which they live. - **C: A community of organisms together with the environment in which they live** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: The flora and fauna of a geographical area** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The fundamental object of Panchayati Raj system is to ensure which among the following?  
1. People's participation in development  
2. Political accountability  
3. Democratic decentralization  
4. Financial mobilization  
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1, 2 and 3 only  
(b) 2 and 4 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Panchayati Raj system was introduced primarily to institutionalize local self-governance and empower people at the grassroots level. Its fundamental objects are: 1. People's participation in development: By involving local communities in planning and implementing development programs, Panchayati Raj ensures bottom-up participation. This statement is correct. 3. Democratic decentralization: It aims to decentralize power from the state government to local elected bodies, enabling decision-making closer to the people. This statement is correct. While political accountability (2) and financial mobilization (4) are important aspects and consequences of a well-functioning Panchayati Raj, they are not the primary, fundamental objectives in the same way as people's participation and democratic decentralization are. The core idea is to bring governance closer to the people and allow them a direct say in their local affairs and development.

## Q86. Indian economy, consider the following
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Indian economy, consider the following  
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Indian economy, consider the following  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q86 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-indian-economy-consider-the-following`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Indian economy, consider the following:  
1. Bank rate  
2. Open market operations  
3. Public debt  
4. Public revenue  
Which of the above is/are component/components of Monetary Policy?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2, 3 and 4  
(c) 1 and 2  
(d) 1, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Monetary Policy refers to the actions undertaken by the central bank (Reserve Bank of India in India) to influence the availability and cost of money and credit in an economy to achieve certain economic objectives like price stability, growth, etc. Its key components include: 1. Bank Rate: This is the rate at which the RBI lends money to commercial banks without any security. It is a quantitative tool of monetary policy. 2. Open Market Operations (OMOs): These involve the buying and selling of government securities by the RBI in the open market to control the money supply. This is also a quantitative tool of monetary policy. Public debt (3) and Public revenue (4) are components of Fiscal Policy, which is concerned with government spending and taxation to influence the economy. Fiscal policy is managed by the government (Ministry of Finance), not the central bank (RBI). Therefore, Bank Rate and Open Market Operations are components of Monetary Policy.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — 1 and 2. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: 1, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q087"></a>
## Q87. Inflation in India, which of the following statements is correct?
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Inflation in India, which of the following statements is correct?  
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Inflation in India, which of the following statements is correct?  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q87 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/03_Inflation-Price-Indices-and-Business-Cycles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/03_Inflation-Price-Indices-and-Business-Cycles.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-inflation-in-india-which-of-the-following-statements-is-correct`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to inflation in India, which of the following statements is correct?

(a) Controlling the inflation in India is the responsibility of the Government of India only  
(b) The Reserve Bank of India has no role in controlling the inflation  
(c) Decreased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation  
(d) Increased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Decreased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze each statement: A) Controlling the inflation in India is the responsibility of the Government of India only: This is incorrect. Inflation control is a joint responsibility of both the Government of India (through fiscal policy) and the Reserve Bank of India (through monetary policy). B) The Reserve Bank of India has no role in controlling the inflation: This is incorrect. The RBI plays a crucial role in controlling inflation, primarily through its monetary policy tools like repo rate, reverse repo rate, CRR, SLR, and open market operations. C) Decreased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation: This is correct. Inflation, especially demand-pull inflation, occurs when there is too much money chasing too few goods. By decreasing the money supply (or money circulation) in the economy, the Reserve Bank of India can reduce aggregate demand, which in turn helps in cooling down inflationary pressures. D) Increased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation: This is incorrect. Increased money circulation would generally lead to an increase in demand and exacerbate inflationary pressures, rather than controlling them.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (C) — Decreased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Controlling the inflation in India is the responsibility of the Government of India only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **B: The Reserve Bank of India has no role in controlling the inflation** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.
- **C: Decreased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **D: Increased money circulation helps in controlling the inflation** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option C.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **inflation in india, which of the following statements is correct?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-consider-the-following-countries`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following countries:  
1. China  
2. France  
3. India  
4. Israel  
5. Pakistan  
Which among the above are Nuclear Weapons States as recognized by the Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons, commonly known as Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty (NPT)?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 1, 3, 4 and 5 only  
(c) 2, 4 and 5 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons (NPT) officially recognizes five Nuclear Weapons States (NWS). These are the five countries that possessed nuclear weapons before 1 January 1967, and are also the five permanent members of the United Nations Security Council (UNSC). These five NWS are: United States (US) Russia (then the Soviet Union) United Kingdom (UK) France China Therefore, from the given options, only China (1) and France (2) are recognized Nuclear Weapons States under the NPT. India (3), Israel (4), and Pakistan (5) are known to possess nuclear weapons but are not signatories to the NPT as NWS, nor are they recognized as such by the NPT. They are considered de facto nuclear weapons states outside the NPT framework.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (A) — 1 and 2 only. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **B: 1, 3, 4 and 5 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **C: 2, 4 and 5 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.
- **D: 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **consider the following countries**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q89. Ideal of 'Welfare State' in the Indian Constitution is enshrined in its
**Primary route:** Polity → [Directive Principles](../../topics/polity/directive-principles.md) → Ideal of 'Welfare State' in the Indian Constitution is enshrined in its  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Directive Principles → Ideal of 'Welfare State' in the Indian Constitution is enshrined in its  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q89 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The ideal of 'Welfare State' in the Indian Constitution is enshrined in its

(a) Preamble  
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy  
(c) Fundamental Rights  
(d) Seventh Schedule  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Directive Principles of State Policy**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The ideal of a 'Welfare State' in the Indian Constitution is most prominently enshrined in the Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP), contained in Part IV (Articles 36 to 51) of the Constitution. These principles are non-justiciable guidelines for the state to establish a social and economic democracy, aiming to promote the welfare of the people by securing a social order in which justice—social, economic, and political—shall inform all the institutions of national life. While the Preamble declares India to be a 'Socialist' republic and aims for justice and equality, and Fundamental Rights protect individual liberties, the DPSP specifically lay down the positive obligations of the state to achieve socio-economic goals, thereby realizing the welfare state ideal.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Directive Principles of State Policy. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: Directive Principles of State Policy** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Fundamental Rights** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Seventh Schedule** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **ideal of 'welfare state' in the indian constitution is enshrined in its**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The substitution of steel for wooden ploughs in agricultural production is an example of

(a) labour-augmenting technological progress  
(b) capital-augmenting technological progress  
(c) capital-reducing technological progress  
(d) None of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — capital-augmenting technological progress**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Technological progress can be classified based on how it affects the productivity of different factors of production (labor and capital). When wooden ploughs are substituted with steel ploughs in agricultural production, it implies the adoption of a more advanced, durable, and efficient form of capital. The steel ploughs, being superior, enhance the productivity of the capital input itself. This makes the existing capital more effective or productive, which is termed 'capital-augmenting technological progress'. It is not primarily 'labour-augmenting' because the direct impact is on the tool (capital), not necessarily making labor more productive in isolation. It is definitely not 'capital-reducing' as it involves using more advanced and effective capital.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — capital-augmenting technological progress. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

- **B: capital-augmenting technological progress** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: capital-reducing technological progress** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: None of the above** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

## Q91. There is a Parliamentary System of Government in India because the
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → There is a Parliamentary System of Government in India because the  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → There is a Parliamentary System of Government in India because the  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q91 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-there-is-a-parliamentary-system-of-government-in-india-because-the`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

There is a Parliamentary System of Government in India because the

(a) Lok Sabha is elected directly by the people  
(b) Parliament can amend the Constitution  
(c) Rajya Sabha cannot be dissolved  
(d) Council of Ministers is responsible to the Lok Sabha  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Council of Ministers is responsible to the Lok Sabha**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The defining characteristic and fundamental reason for India having a Parliamentary System of Government is the principle of 'collective responsibility'. This means that the Council of Ministers, headed by the Prime Minister, is collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha (the lower house of Parliament). If the Lok Sabha passes a no-confidence motion against the Council of Ministers, the entire council must resign. Option A (Lok Sabha elected directly by the people) is a feature of a democratic system, but not the defining characteristic of a *parliamentary* system, as presidential systems can also have directly elected legislatures. Option B (Parliament can amend the Constitution) is a feature of a sovereign parliament, common to many systems. Option C (Rajya Sabha cannot be dissolved) is a feature of the bicameral legislature, specific to India, but not the core reason for its parliamentary nature.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Council of Ministers is responsible to the Lok Sabha. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Lok Sabha is elected directly by the people** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: Parliament can amend the Constitution** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Rajya Sabha cannot be dissolved** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Council of Ministers is responsible to the Lok Sabha** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **there is a parliamentary system of government in india because the**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

## Q93. Bio-toilets used by the Indian Railways, consider the following statements
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Biotechnology Fundamentals and DBT Missions](../../topics/science-and-technology/biotechnology-fundamentals-and-dbt-missions.md) → Bio-toilets used by the Indian Railways, consider the following statements  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Biotechnology Fundamentals and DBT Missions → Bio-toilets used by the Indian Railways, consider the following statements  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q93 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-bio-toilets-used-by-the-indian-railways-consider-the-following-statements`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to bio-toilets used by the Indian Railways, consider the following statements:  
1. The decomposition of human waste in the bio-toilets is initiated by a fungal inoculum.  
2. Ammonia and water vapour are the only end products in this decomposition which are released into the atmosphere.  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze the statements regarding bio-toilets used by Indian Railways: 1. The decomposition of human waste in the bio-toilets is initiated by a fungal inoculum: This statement is incorrect. The decomposition in the bio-digester tanks of Indian Railways' bio-toilets is primarily carried out by a consortium of anaerobic bacteria, not fungi. These bacteria convert human waste into biogas and treated water. 2. Ammonia and water vapour are the only end products in this decomposition which are released into the atmosphere: This statement is incorrect. The primary gaseous end product of the anaerobic digestion process is biogas, which mainly consists of methane (CH4) and carbon dioxide (CO2). Treated water is also produced, which is discharged. While some amount of ammonia might be present, it's not the primary gaseous product, nor are ammonia and water vapor the *only* end products released. The main goal is to prevent raw human waste from being discharged onto the tracks and convert it into less harmful byproducts. Since both statements are incorrect, the answer is neither 1 nor 2.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (D) — Neither 1 nor 2. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option D.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **bio-toilets used by the indian railways, consider the following statements**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The problem of international liquidity is related to the non-availability of

(a) goods and services  
(b) gold and silver  
(c) dollars and other hard currencies  
(d) exportable surplus  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — dollars and other hard currencies**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The problem of international liquidity refers to the non-availability or insufficient supply of internationally accepted means of payment required to finance international trade and financial transactions. In the modern global economy, this primarily involves widely accepted and convertible currencies, often referred to as 'hard currencies'. The US Dollar is the most prominent international reserve currency, but other major currencies like the Euro, Japanese Yen, and Pound Sterling also play significant roles. When there's a shortage of these 'dollars and other hard currencies' that are widely accepted for cross-border payments, it creates a problem of international liquidity, hindering global trade and investment flows.

**Statement/item-level result:**

<a id="2015-prelims-gs1-q095"></a>
## Q95. Fuel cells' in which hydrogen-rich fuel and oxygen are used to generate electricity,...
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Electric Vehicles, Batteries and Alternative Fuels](../../topics/science-and-technology/electric-vehicles-batteries-and-alternative-fuels.md) → Fuel cells' in which hydrogen-rich fuel and oxygen are used to generate electricity,...  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Electric Vehicles, Batteries and Alternative Fuels → Fuel cells' in which hydrogen-rich fuel and oxygen are used to generate electricity,...  
**Metadata:** 2015 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q95 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/18_Electric-Vehicles-Batteries-and-Alternative-Fuels.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/18_Electric-Vehicles-Batteries-and-Alternative-Fuels.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-fuel-cells-in-which-hydrogen-rich-fuel-and-oxygen-are-used-to-generate-electricity`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'fuel cells' in which hydrogen-rich fuel and oxygen are used to generate electricity, consider the following statements:  
1. If pure hydrogen is used as a fuel, the fuel cell emits heat and water as by-products.  
2. Fuel cells can be used for powering buildings and not for small devices like laptop computers.  
3. Fuel cells produce electricity in the form of Alternating Current (AC).  
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate the statements about fuel cells: 1. If pure hydrogen is used as a fuel, the fuel cell emits heat and water as by-products: This statement is correct. A primary advantage of hydrogen fuel cells is their clean operation. When pure hydrogen reacts with oxygen, the only by-products are water (H2O) and heat, making them a highly environmentally friendly energy source. 2. Fuel cells can be used for powering buildings and not for small devices like laptop computers: This statement is incorrect. Fuel cells come in various sizes and power outputs. While large fuel cells can indeed power buildings and vehicles, miniature fuel cells are also developed and used for small portable electronic devices like laptops, cell phones, and cameras. 3. Fuel cells produce electricity in the form of Alternating Current (AC): This statement is incorrect. Fuel cells generate electricity through an electrochemical process, similar to batteries. They produce Direct Current (DC) electricity. While DC can be converted to AC using an inverter for specific applications, the direct output of a fuel cell is DC. Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.

**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Painting Traditions → Kalamkari painting refers to

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Kalamkari painting refers to

(a) a hand-painted cotton textile in South India  
(b) a handmade drawing on bamboo handicrafts in North-East India  
(c) a block-painted woollen cloth in Western Himalayan region of India  
(d) a hand-painted decorative silk cloth in North-Western India  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — a hand-painted cotton textile in South India**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Kalamkari is a traditional Indian art form that involves hand-painting or block-printing on cotton fabric. The name 'Kalamkari' is derived from 'kalam' (pen) and 'kari' (craftsmanship), referring to the use of a pen for drawing outlines and filling in colors. This art form primarily flourishes in parts of South India, especially in Andhra Pradesh and Telangana (e.g., Srikalahasti and Machilipatnam styles). The dyes used are natural, extracted from plants and minerals.

- **D: a hand-painted decorative silk cloth in North-Western India** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option A.

## Q97. Best describes the main objective of 'Seed Village Concept'?
**Primary route:** Economy → [Agricultural Technology Missions and Mission-Mode Policy](../../topics/economy/agricultural-technology-missions-and-mission-mode-policy.md) → Best describes the main objective of 'Seed Village Concept'?  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Agricultural Technology Missions and Mission-Mode Policy → Best describes the main objective of 'Seed Village Concept'?

**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/29_Agricultural-Technology-Missions-and-Mission-Mode-Policy.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/29_Agricultural-Technology-Missions-and-Mission-Mode-Policy.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-best-describes-the-main-objective-of-seed-village-concept`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following best describes the main objective of 'Seed Village Concept'?

(a) Encouraging the farmers to use their own farm seeds and discouraging them to buy the seeds from others  
(b) Involving the farmers for training in quality seed production and thereby to make available quality seeds to others at appropriate time and affordable cost  
(c) Earmarking some villages exclusively for the production of certified seeds  
(d) Identifying the entrepreneurs in villages and providing them technology and finance to set up seed companies  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Involving the farmers for training in quality seed production and thereby to make available quality seeds to others at appropriate time and affordable cost**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The main objective of the 'Seed Village Concept' is to promote the production of quality seeds by farmers themselves at the village level. It aims to empower farmers by training them in scientific methods of seed production, processing, and storage. The certified seeds produced by these trained farmers can then be used for their own cultivation and also supplied to other farmers in the same or nearby villages, ensuring timely availability of quality seeds at affordable prices. This reduces dependency on external sources and promotes self-sufficiency in seed production. Options A, C, and D describe partial aspects or misrepresent the core objective of community-based quality seed production and distribution.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **Official keyed answer:** (B) — Involving the farmers for training in quality seed production and thereby to make available quality seeds to others at appropriate time and affordable cost. The propositions, institutional associations, chronology or factual pairs are resolved by the specific reasoning above; every competing code fails wherever it includes a rejected item or omits a required one.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Encouraging the farmers to use their own farm seeds and discouraging them to buy the seeds from others** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **B: Involving the farmers for training in quality seed production and thereby to make available quality seeds to others at appropriate time and affordable cost** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the controlling facts explained above.
- **C: Earmarking some villages exclusively for the production of certified seeds** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.
- **D: Identifying the entrepreneurs in villages and providing them technology and finance to set up seed companies** — Incorrect: it conflicts with the controlling facts above or, for a statement-code item, includes an incorrect proposition/omits a correct proposition relative to option B.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive fact in **best describes the main objective of 'seed village concept'?**, test each statement, pair or institutional association independently, and remove every option contradicted by that fact. Treat elimination as corroboration, not proof.  
**UPSC trap:** A plausible near-association, wrong institution/location, absolute qualifier, reversed chronology, or partly correct statement is embedded in a competing option.
